# The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Astrology <note>original: "Jyotisha Kalpataru"</note>

->The <gloss>Brief</gloss> Essence of Astrology<- <note>original: "Jyotisha-[alpa]-sara"; the gloss "lpa" appears to modify the title to 'The Brief Essence' or perhaps refers back to the 'Kalpa' in the main title.</note>

<summary>This page is the handwritten cover leaf of a Sanskrit astrological manuscript, identifying the work as "The Wish-Fulfilling Tree of Astrology" (Jyotisha Kalpataru) and its summary version. It includes archival notations regarding its length of 48 leaves and a transcription date from the early 1930s.</summary>

<keywords>Astrology, Jyotisha Kalpataru, Jyotishasara, Sanskrit manuscript, Raghunatha Temple collection, Vikrama Samvat 1990, Divination</keywords>

# Regarding Defects, Regions, Charity, Disease, and Death; the Observation of Vows and Scriptural Learning

<margin>Astro.
1</margin>

Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Ganesha. <note>original: "ॐ श्री गणेशाय नमः"; Ganesha is the elephant-headed deity invoked at the start of any new endeavor to remove obstacles.</note> The Sixty Years <note>original: "Samvatsaras"; a 60-year cycle where each year has a specific name and astrological character.</note> are: 1. Birth <note>Prabhava</note>, 2. Magnificence <note>Vibhava</note>, 3. Brightness <note>Shukla</note>, 4. Joy <note>Pramoda</note>, 5. Lord of Creatures <note>Prajapati</note>, 6. The Celestial Messenger <note>Angira</note>, 7. Divine Countenance <note>Srimukha</note>, 8. Being <note>Bhava</note>, 9. Youth <note>Yuva</note>, 10. The Creator <note>Dhata</note>, 11. The Supreme Lord <note>Isvara</note>, 12. Abundant Grain <note>Bahudhanya</note>, 13. The Madman <note>Pramathi</note>, 14. Valor <note>Vikrama</note>, 15. The Bull <note>Vrisha</note>, 16. Multicolored Splendor <note>Chitrabhanu</note>, 17. Great Splendor <note>Subhanu</note>, 18. The Savior <note>Tarana</note>, 19. Of the Earth <note>Parthiva</note>, 20. Decay <note>Vyaya</note>, 21. Conqueror of All <note>Sarvajit</note>, 22. Sustainer of All <note>Sarvadhari</note>, 23. Non-Opposing <note>Virodhi</note>, 24. Transformation <note>Vikriti</note>, 25. The Harsh One <note>Khara</note>, 26. Delight <note>Nandana</note>, 27. Victory <note>Vijaya</note>, 28. Triumph <note>Jaya</note>, 29. The Love-God <note>Manmatha</note>, 30. The Evil-Faced <note>Durmukha</note>, 31. Golden Support <note>Hemalamba</note>, 32. Delay <note>Vilamba</note>, 33. The Changeful <note>Vikari</note>, 34. Night <note>Sharvari</note>, 35. The Float <note>Plava</note>, 36. The Auspicious Maker <note>Shubhakrit</note>, 37. The Angry One <note>Krodhi</note>, 38. Lord of All Wealth <note>Vishvavasu</note>, 39. Defeat <note>Parabhava</note>, 40. The Leaper <note>Plavanga</note>, 41. The Pin <note>Kilaka</note>, 42. Gentle <note>Saumya</note>, 43. Ordinary <note>Sadharana</note>, 44. Creator of Opposition <note>Virodhakrit</note>, 45. The Runner <note>Paridhavi</note>, 46. Negligence <note>Pramadi</note>, 47. Joy of Vikramaditya <note>Vikananda</note>, 48. The Demon <note>Rakshasa</note>, 49. Fire <note>Anala</note>, 50. The Tawny One <note>Pingala</note>, 51. Bound by Time <note>Kalayukta</note>, 52. Attained Perfection <note>Siddhartha</note>, 53. The Terrible <note>Raudra</note>, 54. Hard to Face <note>Durmukhi</note>, 55. Kettle-Drum <note>Dundubhi</note>, 56. Spouting Blood <note>Rudhirodgari</note>, 57. Red-Eyed <note>Raktakshi</note>, 58. Wrathful <note>Krodhana</note>, <margin>Ru</margin> 59. Decay <note>Kshaya</note>. Thus ends the 60 <term>Samvatsaras</term> <note>These years are used in Hindu astrology to predict the general quality of world events.</note>. 

Next, the twenty-seven <term>Yogas</term> <note>Astrological configurations based on the sum of the positions of the Sun and the Moon.</note>: 1. Support <note>Vishkambha</note>, 2. Affection <note>Priti</note>, 3. Long-Lived <note>Ayushman</note>, 4. Good Fortune <note>Saubhagya</note>, 5. Splendid <note>Shobhana</note>, 6. Severe Knot <note>Atiganda</note>, 7. Good Deed <note>Sukarma</note>, 8. Resolution <note>Dhriti</note>, 9. The Spear <note>Shula</note>, 10. The Knot <note>Ganda</note>, 11. Growth <note>Vriddhi</note>, 12. Constant <note>Dhruva</note>, 13. Obstruction <note>Vyaghata</note>, 14. Joy <note>Harshana</note>, 15. Thunderbolt <note>Vajra</note>, 16. Accomplishment <note>Siddhi</note>, 17. Calamity <note>Vyatipata</note>, 18. Excellent <note>Variyan</note>, 19. Circumference <note>Paridha</note>, 20. Auspicious <note>Shiva</note>, 21. Perfected <note>Siddha</note>, 22. Practicable <note>Sadhya</note>, 23. Fortunate <note>Shubha</note>, 24. White <note>Shukla</note>, 25. Spiritual <note>Brahma</note>, 26. Kingly <note>Aindra</note>, and 27. Total Misfortune <note>Vaidhriti</note>. Thus ends the 27 Yogas.

<summary>This page serves as the formal opening of an astrological treatise, beginning with an invocation to Ganesha and followed by a comprehensive list of the sixty years of the Hindu Jovian cycle (Samvatsaras) and the twenty-seven astrological combinations (Yogas).</summary>

--- Page 4 ---
<meta>This page continues the foundational astrological and calendrical definitions from the previous leaf. Having listed the sixty-year cycle, the text now transitions into the primary celestial bodies, the divisions of the lunar day, the zodiac signs, and the deities governing the lunar mansions.</meta>

The Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, Jupiter, Venus, and Saturn <note>original: "सूर्येन्दुकुजसौम्यसुरगुरुशुक्रशनैश्चराः"; these are the seven visible planets of traditional Indian astronomy, known as the *Grahas*.</note>. 

The *Karanas* <note>original: "करण"; a *Karana* is half of a lunar day (*tithi*). There are eleven types in total, seven of which rotate.</note> are: 1. Bava, 2. Balava, 3. Kaulava, 4. Taitila, 5. Gara, 6. Vanija, and 7. Bhadra.

The Zodiac Signs <note>original: "राशयः" (*Rashis*); the twelve 30-degree segments of the ecliptic.</note> are:
1. Aries <note>Mesha</note>, 2. Taurus <note>Vrishabha</note>, 3. Gemini <note>Mithuna</note>, 4. Cancer <note>Karka</note>, 5. Leo <note>Simha</note>, 6. Virgo <note>Kanya</note>, 7. Libra <note>Tula</note>, 8. Scorpio <note>Vrishchika</note>, 9. Sagittarius <note>Dhanu</note>, 10. Capricorn <note>Makara</note>, 11. Aquarius <note>Kumbha</note>, and 12. Pisces <note>Meena</note>.

### The Deities of the Lunar Mansions
<note>The text lists the presiding divine powers for the 27 *Nakshatras* or lunar houses, starting from the first station, Ashwini.</note>

From the first mansion: The Twin Physicians <note>Ashvins</note>, the God of Death <note>Yama</note>, the Fire God <note>Agni</note>, the Creator <note>Prajapati</note>, the Moon <note>Soma</note>, the Destroyer <note>Rudra</note>, the Mother of Gods <note>Aditi</note>, the Preceptor of Gods <note>Brihaspati</note>, the Serpents <note>Sarpas</note>, the Ancestors <note>Pitrs</note>, the God of Fortune <note>Bhaga</note>, the God of Patronage <note>Aryaman</note>, the Sun <note>Savitr</note>, the Celestial Architect <note>Tvashtr</note>, the Wind God <note>Vayu</note>, the Dual Gods of Lightning and Fire <note>Indra-Agni</note>, the God of Friendship <note>Mitra</note>, the King of Gods <note>Indra</note>, the Goddess of Destruction <note>Nirriti</note>, the Cosmic Waters <note>Apah</note>, the Universal Gods <note>Vishvedevas</note>, the Pervader <note>Vishnu</note>, the Eight Treasurers <note>Vasus</note>, the Lord of Waters <note>Varuna</note>, the One-Footed Goat <note>Aja Ekapada</note>, the Serpent of the Deep <note>Ahirbudhnya</note>, and the Nourisher <note>Pushan</note>.

### On the Commencement of Education
<note>original: "अथ विद्यारंभनक्षत्राणि"; this section provides the electional astrology for when a student should begin their formal studies.</note>

For the beginning of education, the most excellent stars are: Mrigashira and the four following it, Swati, Mula, Hasta and the two following it, Ashwini, and the three "Prior" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>. Commencing studies under these stars brings peace, success, long life, prosperity, <unclear>health</unclear>, intelligence, progeny, power, and victory. Beginning education on a Sunday or other favorable days yields the most excellent fruits. <unclear>These five factors ensure a favorable time.</unclear>

<summary>This page catalogs the fundamental units of the Hindu calendar, including the seven planets, seven rotating Karanas, and twelve zodiac signs. It also lists the presiding deities of the 27 Nakshatras and identifies auspicious lunar mansions for starting one's education.</summary>
<keywords>Grahas, Karanas, Rashis, Nakshatras, Vidya-arambha, electional astrology, Hindu calendar, deities</keywords>

Astrology <note>original: "ज्यो"; abbreviation for *Jyotisha*</note>

One remembers white garments for the mansion of Dhanishta. For conch-white, gold-colored, coral, and red garments, the mansions of Rohini, the five "Hand" stars <note>original: "करपंचके" (*Kara-panchake*); the cluster of five Nakshatras including Hasta</note>, the three "Latter" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Revati are auspicious. On the day of Venus <note>Friday</note>, **wearing new clothes** <note>original: "नववस्त्रपरिधान" (*Navavastra-paridhana*); a ritual timing to ensure the longevity and blessing of new attire</note> is desired.

Regarding the wearing of new clothes: One should avoid daily travel during the three "Former" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Jyeshtha, Bharani, the mansion of one's birth, Krittika, the Serpent mansion <note>Ashlesha</note>, Swati, and Vishakha. 

One should also avoid travel during Chitra, Anuradha, Ardra, and the mansions ruled by Yama <note>Bharani</note>, Fire <note>Krittika</note>, the Ancestors <note>Magha</note>, and the Serpents <note>Ashlesha</note>. The lunar days known as "Empty" <note>original: "रिक्ता" (*Rikta*); the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unsuitable for new ventures</note>, the twelfth day, the sixth day, and the birth mansion must be abandoned for travel. 

Prohibited for travel are Saturdays, Mondays, and Wednesdays; the eighth lunar day, the *Vishti* period <note>an inauspicious astrological "block" or *Karana*</note>, the transition of the Sun <note>Sankranti</note>, and when the Lion <note>Leo</note> is the rising sign <note>Simha-lagna</note>. The ninth lunar day is also - - - - [text lost]. 

For the three "Latter" mansions, Shravana, and Rohini, one should avoid these in both - - - - [text lost]. It is said by the masters of the stars that in the mansions of Rudra <note>Ardra</note>, Mula, and Bharani, things cast away or lost are not recovered. In the mansions of Mula, Ardra, and Bharani, on the second, seventh, ninth, fourteenth, sixteenth, twenty-first, twenty-third, and twenty-eighth days from the Sun's position <note>this refers to *Latta* or *Vedha*, types of astrological obstructions caused by the Sun's planetary influence</note>...

<summary>This page outlines the electional astrology (Muhurta) for daily activities, specifically identifying which lunar mansions and days are auspicious for wearing new clothing and which are forbidden for embarking on journeys.</summary>

### Calculations for Planetary Influence

In the division of the remainder by seven <note>original: "त्रंसप्तभक्तावशेषके" (*Tri-sapta-bhakta-avasheshake*); a common method in Jyotisha to determine the dominant planetary influence for a specific time or person</note>, the positions are stated as three, six, or eight, proceeding from the **Sun and the other stars** <note>the traditional order of the seven visible planets starting with the Sun</note> in their proper sequence. There are four inner stars and three outer stars traditionally proclaimed.

Thus the calculation is to be performed: 
*   The **Sun** <note>original: "रवि" (*Ravi*)</note> represents the tenth portion.
*   The **Moon** <note>original: "हिमराश्मि" (*Himaraśmi* — literally "cool-rayed one")</note> represents the sixteenth portion.
*   **Mercury** <note>original: "बुध" (*Budha*)</note> is the third portion.
*   **Saturn** <note>original: "शनि" (*Shani*)</note> is the eighth part.
*   **Mars** <note>original: "धरासुतो" (*Dharasuto* — literally "son of the Earth")</note> is the twelfth part. 

The divisors for **Jupiter** and **Venus** <note>original: "गुरुशुक्रवारौ" (*Guru-shukra-varau*)</note> follow. The stars presided over by the Lord of Creatures <note>Rohini</note>, Shravana, the three "Latter" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Aditi <note>Punarvasu</note>, and the Sun's own stars <note>Hasta</note>, along with Vishnu <note>Shravana</note>, Ashvini, and Pushan <note>Revati</note> are considered. So too are Shatabhishak, Swati, and Vishakha.

### On Agriculture and Sowing

When **Jupiter** <note>original: "जीवा" (*Jiva*)</note> is in a quadrant <note>original: "कैंद्र" (*Kendra*); the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart, which are considered the strongest pillars of a horoscope</note>, and on the days of Venus and Mercury <note>original: "सोम्ये" (*Somye*); another name for Mercury as the son of the Moon</note>, during a favorable rising sign <note>original: "लग्नोदये" (*Lagnodaye*)</note>:

One is empowered for the **sowing of crops** <note>original: "सस्यानांवपने" (*Sasyanam-vapane*)</note> as well as for the **harvesting** <note>original: "लवने" (*Lavane*)</note>. For the collection of seeds, one should always choose the mansion of Hasta. The threshing of grain <note>original: "मर्द्दनं" (*Mardanam*)</note> should be performed under the star Rohini.

<margin>
Sowing <note>original: "वाप"; abbreviation for *Vapana*</note>
</margin>

### Marriage and Domestic Life

For the entry into a home, or for the following auspicious acts:
1.  **Leading the bride** <note>original: "आवाहे" (*Avahe*)</note>
2.  **The marriage ceremony** <note>original: "विवाहे" (*Vivahe*)</note>
3.  **Choosing a maiden** <note>original: "कन्योसंवरणो" (*Kanyo-samvarano*)</note>

The following stars are auspicious: Mrigashira, Hasta, Swati, Mula, Anuradha, Revati, Rohini, and the three "Latter" mansions. Under these same stars, one should **sow all types of seeds** and perform the **entry into a house or a village** <note>original: "गृहग्रामंप्रवेशयेत्" (*Griha-gramam-praveshayet*); highlighting that the founding or moving into a new community required the same celestial sanction as a private home</note>.

<summary>This page details the mathematical portions of planetary influence and lists the specific lunar mansions (Nakshatras) favorable for agricultural activities like sowing and harvesting, as well as domestic rituals including marriage and house-warming.</summary>
<keywords>Muhurta, agriculture, sowing, marriage, house entry, Nakshatra, Jupiter, Venus, Rohini, Hasta, planetary portions</keywords>

Having discussed planetary influences and transitions on the previous page, the author now provides specific auspicious timings (Muhurtas) for agricultural activities such as harvesting, sowing, and the first plowing of the season.</meta>

<page-num>3</page-num>
<header>Astrology <note>original: "ज्यो·" (*Jyo*), abbreviation for *Jyotisha*</note></header>

<margin>Astrology 3</margin>

**Pushya**, **Anuradha** <note>original: "मैत्रं" (*Maitram*), the star presided over by the deity Mitra</note>, **Shravana**, **Dhanishtha** <note>original: "वसाभिः" (*Vasabhih*), referring to the Vasus who rule this star</note>, **Revati** <note>original: "पौष्णं" (*Paushnam*), ruled by Pushan</note>, **Ashvini** <note>original: "दस्रं" (*Dasram*), referring to the Ashvins</note>, **Hasta**, **Swati**, **Punarvasu** <note>original: "दित्य" (*Aditya*), referring to the goddess Aditi</note>, and **Mula** <note>original: "निर्ऋति" (*Nirriti*), the deity of this star</note>.

On the day of **Jupiter**, the teacher of the gods <note>original: "गुरुदेवपूज्येच वारे" (*Gurudevapujye cha vare*); i.e., Thursday</note>, these lunar mansions are favorable. The **cutting of grain** <note>original: "धान्य छेदा" (*Dhanya cheda*); harvesting</note> performed at these times is declared by the Sages to be **grain-increasing** <note>original: "धान्य वृद्धिकरी" (*Dhanya vriddhikari*)</note>.

### On Sowing Seeds

The lunar mansions for **sowing seeds** <note>original: "वीजनक्षत्राणि" (*Vija-nakshatrani*)</note> are:
**Hasta**, **Pushya**, the three **Latter** mansions <note>original: "उत्तरी" (*Uttara*); likely referring to Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Rohini**, **Chitra**, **Anuradha**, **Mrigashirsha**, **Revati**, **Swati**, **Dhanishtha**, **Magha**, and **Mula**. Sowing during these stars is declared to yield abundant fruit.

### On the First Plowing

The **first plowing** <note>original: "हल प्रवाह प्रथमं" (*Hala pravaha prathamam*)</note> should be performed during the "soft" or "fixed" lunar mansions, or the "movable" ones, specifically including **Mula**, **Magha**, and **Vishakha**. This should be done when one is **free from disease** <note>original: "रोग मुक्ता" (*Roga mukta*)</note>.

Regarding the **Bull diagram** <note>original: "सौरभेयो" (*Saurabheyo*); an astrological chart where stars are mapped onto the body of a bull to predict the harvest</note>, the stars are calculated in groups: three, three, three, five, three, five, and three.

In this **plowing calculation**:
*   **Three** stars are placed in the **head** <note>original: "मूर्ध्न" (*Murdhna*)</note>.
*   **Three** in the **throat** <note>original: "गले" (*Gale*)</note>.
*   **Twelve** stars reside in the **belly** <note>original: "जठरे" (*Jathare*)</note>.
*   **Four** and **two** are placed externally.

Beyond these positions, the remaining stars indicate the outcome. Through this sequence, one determines the emergence of wealth and the abundance of grain <note>original: "तं ड लत्वं" (*Tandulatvam*); literally "rice-ness" or yield</note> resulting from the influence of the demons and deities.

<summary>This page lists auspicious lunar mansions (Nakshatras) for agricultural tasks, specifically harvesting and sowing seeds. It also introduces the "Hala Chakra" or Bull Diagram, a method of mapping stars to a bull's body to determine the best time for the first plowing of the year.</summary>
<keywords>Jyotisha, Nakshatras, harvesting, seed sowing, plowing, Hala Chakra, agriculture, Thursday, lunar mansions</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical astrological instructions. While the previous page focused on auspicious timings for agriculture (harvesting and sowing), this page shifts focus to "Yogas"—specific alignments between the day of the week and the lunar mansions (Nakshatras) that determine the overall success or danger of any undertaking.</meta>

### The Circle of Fruitfulness <note>original: "फलीचक्रं" (*Phali-chakram*)</note>

The following are the **Alignments of Perfect Nectar** <note>original: "अमृतसिद्धयोगाः" (*Amrita-siddha-yogah*); these are highly auspicious times when actions are said to achieve "immortal" success</note>:
- **Sunday** and the star **Hasta**
- **Thursday** and the star **Pushya**
- **Wednesday** and the star **Anuradha**
- **Saturday** and the star **Rohini**
- **Monday** and the star **Mrigashirsha** <note>original: "सौम्यं" (*Saumyam*), the "gentle" star ruled by the Moon</note>
- **Friday** and the star **Revati**
- **Tuesday** and the star **Ashvini**

### The Alignments of Death

Beginning from **Sunday**, the four stars starting with **Vishakha** result in the **Yoga of Death** <note>original: "मृत्यु योग" (*Mrityu Yoga*)</note> or the **Deficient Alignment** <note>original: "काण योग" (*Kana Yoga*); literally the "one-eyed" or lopsided yoga</note>, though some may also result in a **Success Alignment** <note>original: "सिद्धयोगो" (*Siddha-yogo*)</note> depending on specific calculations.

According to the Sages, the following combinations are specifically called **Death Alignments** <note>original: "मृत्यु योगा" (*Mrityu Yoga*)</note>:
- **Monday** and the star **Anuradha**
- **Tuesday** and the star **Uttara Ashadha**
- **Wednesday** and the star **Shatabhisha**
- **Thursday** and the star **Ardra**
- **Friday** and the star **Ashvini**
- **Saturday** <note>original: "रविसुत्त" (*Ravisuta*); literally "Son of the Sun," a name for Saturn</note> and the star **Hasta**

<margin>Note</margin> It is said that:
- On **Sunday** and the star **Bharani**
- On **Monday** and the star **Rohini** <note>original: "ब्राह्ये" (*Brahye*); the star ruled by Brahma</note>
- On **Tuesday** and the star **Uttara Ashadha** <margin>Cont.</margin>
- On **Wednesday** and the star **Dhanishtha**
- On **Thursday** and the star **Uttara Phalguni**
- On **Friday** and the star **Jyeshtha**
- On **Saturday** <note>original: "सूर्यपुत्रे" (*Suryaputre*); "Son of the Sun," referring to Saturn</note> and the star **Revati**

One should **not perform any auspicious work** <note>original: "शुभकर्म न कारयेत्" (*Shubhakarma na karayet*)</note> during these times. 

### The Consequences of Ill-Timed Actions

If one proceeds during these inauspicious alignments:
- In **Marriage**, it leads to certain **widowhood**.
- In **Travel** <note>original: "प्रवासं" (*Pravasam*)</note>, it leads to certain **death**.
- In the **Commencement of Education** <note>original: "विद्यारंभे" (*Vidyarambhe*)</note>, the result will be **foolishness** <note>original: "मूर्खत्वं" (*Murkhatvam*)</note>.

Conversely, for **Marriage**, the alignments of **Thursday-Pushya**, **Thursday-Shravana**, **Saturday-Rohini**, or **Tuesday-Ashvini** are considered firm. For **Entering a Home**, the three stars... <note>The text cuts off here as it transitions to rules for the fifth and sixth lunar days</note> **Sunday on the fifth day** with **Hasta**, or on the **sixth day**...

<summary>This page defines Amrita Siddha Yoga (auspicious) and Mrityu Yoga (inauspicious) alignments between weekdays and Nakshatras. It warns that performing major life events like marriage, travel, or starting school during "Death Alignments" leads to disastrous outcomes.</summary>

### The Alignments of the Staff of Death <note>original: "कालदण्ड" (*Kaladanda*); literally the "Staff of Time" or the "Rod of Death," symbolizing a period of punishment or inevitable failure</note>

The following specific combinations are known as the **Staff of Death**:
- **Monday** <note>original: "सोम" (*Soma*)</note> when the Moon is in the star **Shravana**.
- **Tuesday** <note>original: "भौम" (*Bhauma*)</note> when it is the **7th lunar day** and the star is **Ashvini**.
- **Tuesday** when it is the **8th lunar day** and the star is **Anuradha**.
- **Thursday** <note>original: "गुरु" (*Guru*)</note> when the star is **Pushya** and it is the **9th lunar day**.
- **Friday** <note>original: "भृगु" (*Bhrigu*)</note> when the star is **Revati** and it is the **10th lunar day**.
- **Saturday** <note>original: "शनि" (*Shani*)</note> when the star is **Rohini** and it is the **11th lunar day**.

### The Burnt Alignments <note>original: "दग्धयोग" (*Dagdhayoga*); "burnt" or "scorched" alignments where the auspicious energy of the day is consumed by the heat of the lunar date</note>

These are the **Burnt Alignments** which render any action fruitless:
- **Wednesday** <note>original: "बुध" (*Budha*)</note> on the **3rd lunar day**.
- **Tuesday** <note>original: "कुज" (*Kuja*)</note> on the **5th lunar day**.
- **Thursday** on the **6th lunar day**.
- **Sunday** <note>original: "अर्क" (*Arka*)</note> on the **8th or 11th lunar day**.
- **Monday** and **Saturday** on the **9th lunar day**.
- **Sunday** on the **12th lunar day**.

### The Flaming Mouth <note>original: "ज्वालामुखो" (*Jvalamukhi*); literally "Fire-faced," these are considered extremely volatile and destructive timings</note>

The following are recorded as the **Flaming Mouth** alignments:
- The **4th lunar day** joined with the **Uttara** stars <note>referring to the "latter" stars: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, or Uttara Bhadrapada</note>.
- The **5th lunar day** joined with the star **Magha**.
- The **2nd lunar day** joined with the star **Shravana**.
- The **9th lunar day** joined with the star **Krittika**.
- The **8th lunar day** joined with the star **Rohini**.

One must **abandon all important works** during these times, as they are **stealers of life-breath** <note>original: "प्राणहराणि" (*Pranaharani*); implies they bring immediate misfortune or danger to one's vital energy</note>.

### The Calculation of the Spirit-Goddesses <note>original: "योगिनीषु" (*Yogini*); refers to the directional placement of the sixty-four Yogini goddesses who govern the success of journeys and rituals</note>

Using the numerical codes of the Sages <note>original: "मन्वर्कदिग्वसवो" (*Manvarka-dig-vasavo*); these are mnemonic numbers: 14, 12, 10, and 8</note> and the four Vedas, one calculates the movement of the **Yoginis**. When these spirits are encountered "head-on" or in a conflicting direction, they are **prohibited for auspicious deeds**.

### Forbidden Three-Hour Watches <note>original: "प्रहरा" (*Prahara*); a day and night are divided into eight watches of three hours each</note>

Certain three-hour intervals are strictly forbidden on specific days:
- On **Tuesday**: the **2nd watch** <note>approx. 9:00 AM – 12:00 PM</note>.
- On **Aja** <note>likely referring to the star of the 1st sign (Aries) or Friday in this context</note>: the **3rd watch** <note>approx. 12:00 PM – 3:00 PM</note>.
- On **Sunday**: the **4th watch** <note>approx. 3:00 PM – 6:00 PM</note>.
- On **Wednesday**: the **5th watch** <note>approx. 6:00 PM – 9:00 PM</note>.
- On **Saturday**: the **6th watch** <note>approx. 9:00 PM – 12:00 AM</note>.
- On **Monday** <note>original: "इन्दुवारे" (*Indu-vare*)</note>: the **7th watch** <note>approx. 12:00 AM – 3:00 AM</note>.
- On **Thursday** <note>original: "जीवे" (*Jive*)</note>: the **8th watch** <note>approx. 3:00 AM – 6:00 AM</note>.

During these forbidden watches, all actions are prohibited.

### The Direction of the Moon <note>original: "चन्द्र निवास" (*Chandra Nivasa*); the residence or facing direction of the Moon, crucial for determining the success of a journey</note>

When the Sun is in **Aries**, **Leo**, or **Sagittarius**, the Moon resides in the **East**.
When in **Virgo** or **Capricorn**, it resides in the **South** <note>original: "याम्ये" (*Yamye*); referring to Yama, the lord of the South</note>.
When in **Aquarius**, **Libra**, or **Gemini** <note>original: "मन्मथ" (*Manmatha*); a poetic synonym for the sign Gemini</note>, it resides in the **West**.
When in **Cancer**, **Scorpio** <note>original: "अलि" (*Ali*); literally "scorpion"</note>, or **Pisces**, the Moon is in the **North**.

The effects of these positions are as follows:
- If the Moon is **facing you** <note>original: "सन्मुख" (*Sammukha*)</note>, it brings great gain and all forms of prosperity.
- If the Moon is **behind you**, it brings death or significant loss.
- If the Moon is to your **left**, it bestows wealth.
- If to the **right** <note>interpreted from "दोषं" (*Dosham*) in context of standard direction rules</note>, it causes obstacles or loss.

### The Power to Overcome Afflictions

The specific defects of the day, such as those arising from the day of the week or various planetary alignments, or even the defect of the **ninth lunar day**, are neutralized. The Sun, Moon, Mars, Mercury, and Jupiter <note>original: "रवि शशि कुज सौम्या च बुध"</note> acting individually might not remove all faults, but the **Moon joined with the star Rohini** destroys all defects. <unclear>७</unclear>

### The Excellence of the Star Pushya <note>original: "पुष्य" (*Pushya*); often called the "King of Stars," considered the most auspicious for starting new ventures</note>

Regarding the avoidance of loss: the star **Pushya** is like a jewel among the mansions. Even if the Moon is in the **Twelfth House** <note>original: "द्वादशों चंद्रे" (*Dvadashon Chandre*); usually considered an unlucky or weakening position for the Moon</note>, Pushya remains the **accomplisher of all goals**. 

When the Sun is in **Leo**, Pushya grants all desired fruits. Pushya is inherently powerful. Even when the Moon’s position is otherwise unfavorable, any tasks performed during the influence of Pushya will reach a successful completion.

### The Five-Fold Affliction <note>original: "पंचक" (*Panchaka*); a period of five days when certain stars are active, often considered inauspicious for specific activities like traveling south or building roofs</note>

Now, the **Panchaka** [is described]:

<margin>Jyotisha 5th [Folio] Day</margin> From the eighth day of the dark fortnight of the month of **Vaishakha** <note>original: "वैशाखकृष्णाष्टमि" (*Vaishakha-krishna-ashtami*); Vaishakha usually falls in April–May</note>, there arise five well-known **Pishachas** <note>original: "पिशाचकानि" (*Pishachkani*); here referring to specific malevolent astrological influences or spirits</note>.

It is said these bring inauspiciousness to the world; a woman married during this time may become a widow.

During the period of the **Panchaka** <note>original: "पंचके" (*Panchake*); a group of five stars—Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati—considered unfavorable for specific tasks</note> when the Moon is in the southern direction, one must avoid:
- The storage of wealth or secret things
- The cremation of the dead <note>original: "प्रेत दाह" (*Preta daha*)</note>
- The gathering of wood
- The making of beds

He who is bitten by a snake <note>original: "भुजंगदष्ट्राः" (*Bhujanga-dashtrah*)</note> under the stars of **Krittika**, **Mula**, **Magha**, **Vishakha**, or the stars associated with the Serpent <note>original: "सार्पा" (*Sarpa*); referring to the star Ashlesha</note> or Yama <note>original: "अन्त" (*Anta*); referring to Bharani</note>, that man will surely meet death.

When a disease originates <note>original: "रोगोत्पत्ति" (*Rogotpatti*)</note> under the stars of **Swati**, **Ashlesha**, **Ardra** <note>original: "रौद्र" (*Raudra*); the fierce star associated with Rudra</note>, or the **Purvas**, it takes hold of a man. In such cases, despite all efforts and even the presence of a skilled **physician** <note>original: "वैद्य" (*Vaidya*)</note>, the illness is difficult to overcome. 

If the disease begins during these times, it often manifests as **eye diseases** <note>original: "नेत्रारोग" (*Netraroga*)</note> or fevers. However, recovery from illness <note>original: "रोग मुक्ति" (*Roga mukti*)</note> is possible when the Moon or Mercury <note>original: "सौम्ये" (*Saumye*)</note> are favorably placed on an auspicious day.

<unclear>४</unclear> Under the influence of **Padma**, **Hasta**, or **Dhanishta** <note>original: "वासव" (*Vasava*)</note>, or when moving through **Mula** or **Ashwini**, the fire of disease may be quenched, or conversely, one might face loss depending on the Twashtra <note>Chitra star</note> or Vasava alignments.

### The Proper Time for Bathing after Illness
Now follows the timing for the ceremonial bath after being freed from disease <note>original: "अथयोगविमुक्तस्नानकालः" (*atha-yoga-vimukta-snana-kalah*); in traditional medicine, the first bath after a fever or long illness was a significant ritual milestone</note>. On a Monday <note>original: "इंदौ" (*Indau*)</note> or a Friday <note>original: "भार्गवे" (*Bhargave*)</note>, and under the stars of **Shravana**, **Pushya**, or **Punarvasu**, or on a Sunday <note>original: "अर्क" (*Arka*)</note> or Wednesday <note>original: "सौम्ये" (*Saumye*)</note>, one should perform the bath. 

One should never take this bath for a person recently freed from disease on a Saturday <note>original: "अर्कजे" (*Arkaje*); literally "Sun-born," an epithet for Saturn</note>, or on the **Rikta** lunar days <note>the "empty" days—the 4th, 9th, and 14th of the lunar fortnight—considered inauspicious for new beginnings</note>, or when the moon is weak.

If one bathes under the stars of **Chitra**, **Dhanistha**, **Ashvini**, or **Krittika** <note>original: "वह्नि" (*Vahni*); the star of the fire god Agni</note>, the disease will not be truly cast off. 

Under the stars of **Hasta**, **Chitra**, or during the movement of the moon through **Bharani** <note>original: "याम्ये" (*Yamye*); the star of Yama, god of death</note> or **Punarvasu**, even if one has bathed and seems freed from the ailment <note>original: "व्याधिनिर्मुक्तः" (*vyadhinirmuktah*)</note>, they may become possessed by the illness once again.

Under **Magha**, **Mula**, **Vishakha**, **Ardra**, the middle of the three stars beginning with **Chitra**, **Jyestha**, **Ashlesha** <note>original: "सर्प" (*Sarpa*); the serpent star</note>, or **Shatabhisha** <note>original: "जलेश्वर" (*Jaleshvara*)</note>, one should absolutely not bathe...

### Calculation of the Five-Fold Evil (Panchaka)
To find the Five-Fold Evil <term>Panchaka; five specific astrological conditions considered harmful for starting new ventures, traveling, or building</term>, take the number of the elapsed lunar day <term>Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</term> and add it to the number of the rising sign <term>Lagna; the zodiac sign ascending on the eastern horizon</term>. Divide this sum by nine <note>original: "नंद" (*nanda*); the number nine is symbolized by the nine Nanda kings of history</note>. 

If the remainder is one of the following, the "Five-fold" influence is present:
*   **Disease** <note>original: "रुग" (*ruga*)</note>
*   **Fire** <note>original: "अनल" (*anala*)</note>
*   **The King** <note>original: "नृप" (*nripa*); representing legal or governmental trouble</note>
*   **The Thief** <note>original: "चोर" (*chora*)</note>
*   **Death** <note>original: "मृत्यु" (*mrityu*)</note>

When these remainders—symbolized by the elephant <note>8</note>, the pair <note>2</note>, the ages <note>4</note>, and the fires <note>3</note>—are combined, the Five-fold Evil is known. One should never perform important work during these times. 

In the same way, using the elapsed lunar day and the rising sign, if the division by nine results in the sequence of Disease, Fire, the King, and the Thief, it is a period of blemish. Death is the final designation. Even for one’s own benefit, one should avoid these.

<insert>Starting from the star of the day, add the numbers of the lunar day and the weekday. Divide this total by seven <note>original: "अंक" (*anka*) is often 9, but here "सप्त" (*sapta*) clearly specifies 7</note>.</insert>

If the remainder is such, it becomes the **Giver of All Success** <term>Sarvasiddhi; a planetary alignment believed to guarantee the successful completion of any task</term>. Taking the star occupied by the Sun as the starting point, multiply the lunar day by three, add them together, and divide by seven. The remaining lunar day <unclear>obtained</unclear> is the Giver of All Success, starting from the Sun’s position.

<meta>This page continues the astrological technical instructions for calculating the "Five-fold Evil" (Panchaka) and transitions into a list of specific "Striking" or harmful astrological influences—known as Ghata—that must be avoided for specific activities like war, construction, and travel.</meta>

Combined with the day, the constellation <term>Nakshatra; one of the 27 lunar mansions</term>, the lunar day <term>Tithi</term>, and the day of the week <term>Vara</term>, the sum is divided by nine <note>original: "नन्द" (*nanda*), a numerical symbol for nine</note>. 

This calculation is the best for granting all success <term>Sarvasiddhida; the giver of all accomplishments</term>. It should be remembered when embarking on a journey <term>Yatra</term>, using mechanical or mystical diagrams <term>Yantra</term>, or during the first plowing of a field <term>Halapravaha</term>. It is also used to detect secret actions or theft.

### Prohibited Times for Specific Actions
In the following matters, one must always avoid the harmful influence known as **Adalo** <note>a specific local or technical name for a malefic period</note>:
*   Constructing or binding stepped-wells <term>Vapi</term>, regular wells <term>Kupa</term>, or ponds <term>Tadaga</term>.
*   Dealing with heretics or seizing a fort <term>Durga</term>.
*   The ritual first riding of horses or elephants.

In other auspicious or celebratory rituals, there is no fault or defect in these times. However, during war, disputes regarding horses, or when capturing a fort under the influence of malefic planets, the **Striking Moon** <term>Ghatachandra; a transit of the moon that is specifically harmful to an individual based on their birth sign</term> must always be avoided. It is not considered harmful in other auspicious works.

### The "Striking" Constellations (Ghata Nakshatras)
For each zodiac sign, starting with Aries, there is a specific constellation that is considered "striking" or harmful <note>original: "घात" (*ghata*)</note>. These should be avoided for significant undertakings:

1.  **Aries**: The Ancestors' star <note>original: "पित्र्य" (*Pitrya*), referring to **Magha**</note>
2.  **Taurus**: The Sun's star <note>original: "सौर्य" (*Saurya*), referring to **Hasta**</note>
3.  **Gemini**: The Wind's star <note>original: "मारुत" (*Maruta*), referring to **Swati**</note>
4.  **Cancer**: The Friend's star <note>original: "मैत्र" (*Maitra*), referring to **Anuradha**</note>
5.  **Leo**: The Root star <note>original: **Mula**</note>
6.  **Virgo**: The Water star <note>original: "वारुण" (*Varuna*), referring to **Shatabhisha**</note>
7.  **Libra**: The Wealthy star <note>original: **Revati**</note>
8.  **Scorpio**: The Restrainer's star <note>original: "याम्य" (*Yamya*), referring to **Bharani**</note>
9.  **Sagittarius**: The Creator's star <note>original: "ब्राह्मय" (*Brahmya*), referring to **Rohini**</note>
10. **Capricorn**: The Terrible star <note>original: "रौद्र" (*Raudra*), referring to **Ardra**</note>
11. **Aquarius**: The Auspicious star <note>original: "तिष्य" (*Tishya*), referring to **Pushya**</note>

### Planetary Placements and Harmful Dates
The Sun <note>original: "अर्क" (*Arka*)</note> in Aries, Saturn <note>original: "मन्द" (*Manda*)</note> in Leo or Virgo... <unclear>Hamsa</unclear>... the sign of Capricorn. Jupiter <note>original: "जीव" (*Jiva*)</note> in Cancer and Aquarius. Venus <note>original: "भार्गव" (*Bhargava*)</note> in Sagittarius. 

The following lunar days <term>Tithis</term> are considered the "lowest of time" or harmful for specific signs:
*   The **Second** and **Sixth** lunar days...
*   The **Fourth** lunar day for Taurus and Aquarius.
*   The **Sixth** lunar day for Aries and Cancer.
*   The... for Virgo and Gemini.

### Calculations for Time and Shadow

For Pisces and Aries, the movement <note>referring to the calculation of time or shadow</note> is three and a half units. For Gemini and Capricorn, it is five. The remainder are declared as three and a half.

Thus concludes the results for the timing of the rising sign <term>Lagna</term>. Regarding the yoga <note>astrological combination</note> for childhood illnesses <term>Balaroga</term>, one should honor the mountain-holder <note>Lord Krishna</note> and Shiva <note>Hara</note>. 

The specific time units <term>Ghatika; a period of 24 minutes</term> for the various signs starting with Aries are calculated in sequence. The duration of the day <term>Dinamanaka</term> for Aries and the other signs is measured according to the length of the foot-shadow <term>Padachhaya; an ancient method of telling time by measuring the length of one's own shadow in "feet"</term>, joined with the numerical codes for the sages and Manu <note>traditional numerical symbols used in Sanskrit astronomical tables</note>.

The stars **Mula**, **Krittika** <note>original: *Agneya*, referring to the deity of fire</note>, **Magha**, **Bharani**, **Ashlesha** <note>original: *Sarpa*, the serpent-deity</note>, and the three **"Prior"** stars <note>original: *Purva-trayam*; these are Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note> are proclaimed by those learned in the stars <term>Jyotishis</term> as the nine "downward-facing" <term>Adhomukha</term> lunar mansions. In these, the construction of stepped-wells <term>Vapi</term>, standard wells <term>Kupa</term>, ponds <term>Tadaga</term>, the digging of ditches and moats, and the uncovering of buried treasure are successful. Furthermore, the lighting of lamps, gambling, the entry of wealth, and the commencement of accounting are accomplished here.

The stars **Jyeshtha**, **Ashwini**, **Mrigashira**, **Revati** <note>original: *Paushna*, referring to the deity Pushan</note>, **Anuradha**, **Krittika** <note>original: *Anala*</note>, and **Chitra** <note>original: *Tvashtra*, the celestial architect</note> are the stars that the ancient sages say <unclear>by this way</unclear> are the "sideways-facing" <term>Tiryagmukha</term> mansions. In these, the training or use of horses, camels, and bulls, as well as matters regarding tusks and teeth, are favorable. The recitation of mantras, the <insert>commencement</insert> of plowing <note>original: *halapravaha*</note>, and the starting of journeys are also successfully accomplished.

**Pushya**, **Ardra**, **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, the three **"Latter"** stars <note>original: *Uttara-trayam*; these are Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Rohini** <note>original: *Brahma*</note>, and **Shravana** are what the sages call the "upward-facing" <term>Urdhvamukha</term> stars... <unclear>by this way</unclear>... In these, the construction of temples or palaces <term>Prasada</term>, the raising of banners, building mansions, installing gates, boundary walls, and ceremonial archways <term>Toranas</term>, and the layout of shaded gardens are beneficial. These stars are also used for the coronation of kings <term>Pattabhisheka</term> and similar rites ————— the infinite blessings of the cows <note>This final phrase is fragmented; "Go-vardhana" may imply the increase of cattle or refer to a specific closing benediction.</note>.

### The Circle of the Bull

If there is one mark on the forehead of the "Cruel-Joy" horned bull <note>original: *Kruranandi-shringa*; likely referring to a specific diagnostic diagram or a specific type of bull used in divination</note>, it signifies profit. Two marks on the teeth are fruitless. Four marks on the eyes bring profit, but four on the horns bring loss. Four on the back lead to profit, and seven on the belly bring gains. Four on the feet are fruitless. Six marks [in one place] and one on the anus are to be known as fruitless; so it is understood by the wise followers of **Gautama**.

Regarding the stars **Punarvasu**, **Mrigashira**, **Pushya**... <note>The text here lists specific lunar mansions used to calculate the "Cow Circle"</note> if the Cow Circle <term>Gava-chakra</term> is consulted: take the initial letter group <term>Varga</term> of a name, multiply it by three, and add the remaining letter groups. Divide this total by eight. From the remainder, the "Calculator" <term>Gani</term> is determined. The results [based on the remainder] are the Eagle <note>original: *Vainateya*, another name for Garuda</note>, Yama <note>the God of Death</note>, the Lion, and the Sound...

Likewise, the "Face" is the Soul <term>Jiva</term>; the Brahmin class <term>Brahma-varga</term> is the King... The letter groups are categorized as **A**, **Ka**, **Cha**, **Ta**, **Ta**, **Pa**, **Ya**, and **Sha** <note>These represent the phonetic rows of the Sanskrit alphabet used in ancient name-divination</note>. When the initial group is placed in three positions, it represents the village. One should avoid the sixth and seventh positions. The third and fifth village [calculations] are where that group is produced; whatever is born there, in that very place it meets its end.

<summary>This page describes the Gava-chakra, a system of divination using physical marks on bulls to predict profit or loss, and the Varga-chakra, a phonetic calculation used to determine the compatibility and fate of a person within a specific village or location.</summary>
<keywords>Gava-chakra, bovine divination, Gautama, Varga, Sanskrit phonetics, village astrology, Kruranandi, omen reading</keywords>

<margin>Astrology <br> 8</margin> Regarding the eighth village <note>Referring to the divinatory calculation of "Varga" or phonetic groups mentioned on the previous page</note>, if it results in the twelfth, eleventh, or tenth positions, there will be **Great Profit** <term>Mahalabha</term>. 

Life is said to be strengthened when the Moon, Venus, and the stars **Dhanishtha**, **Ashwini**, and **Revati** are present. The stars **Chitra**, **Shatabhishak**, **Anuradha**, the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Swati**, and **Hasta** are also auspicious.

When benefic planets and **Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note> are in the Ascendant <term>Lagna</term>, and malefic planets <note>original: *papara-kheta*; literally "sinful planets" like Saturn or Mars</note> are in the third or eleventh houses <note>original: *trilabhe*; houses of gain and effort</note>, there will be an increase in happiness, wealth, and protection; otherwise, there may be "binding" <note>likely referring to legal or physical restriction</note>. 

### The Ritual of Ear-Piercing

The stars **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, **Hasta** <note>original: *Kara*</note>, **Mrigashira**, and **Swati** are favorable. For the days of the week, Monday, Wednesday, Friday, and the Moon's lunar days are considered auspicious. The royal hours <note>original: *Rajiyarakvashubha*</note> during a beneficial Ascendant, hour <term>Hora</term>, or day are best. 

The great sages have stated that the cutting of the hair <note>original: *kesha-khandana*</note> should be avoided [under certain conditions]. According to the sequence of **Rahu** <note>the shadow planet or North Node of the Moon</note>: when the Moon is in **Mrigashira**, **Pushya**, **Chitra**, or **Shravana**, and it is a Saturday, Thursday, or Tuesday, the **Ear-Piercing ceremony** <term>Karnavedha</term> is highly praised. 

Specific quarters <note>original: *pade*; a Nakshatra is divided into four quarters or 'feet'</note> of the stars are specified: 
* The three **Uttaras**.
* Two quarters of **Pushya**.
* Three quarters of **Purva Ashadha**.
* The end of **Chitra**.

Performing the ritual during these times is said to destroy "excess bile" <note>original: *pitta*; one of the three humors in Ayurvedic medicine</note> in the child's constitution.

Of this, there is no doubt. There is one in **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note> and **Aries** <note>original: *Mesha*</note>; there are four in **Taurus** <note>original: *Vrishabha*</note> and **Aquarius** <note>original: *Kumbha*</note>. The wise state that three [points] should be taken. Just as a king's bed <margin>hour</margin> is affected when **Mars** <note>original: *Mangala*</note> causes injury to the body, or just as when the **Sun** <note>original: *Ravi*</note> is seen, so it is with the appearance of a lamp. 

When **Rahu** <note>the shadow planet or North Node</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: *Chandra*</note> are always situated in the sixth house <note>The sixth house in a horoscope is traditionally associated with obstacles and health</note>, death will surely come to that newborn <term>Jataka</term>; of this, there is no doubt.

### The Mapping of the Sun upon the Body

In whichever circle the **Sun** <note>original: *Bhanu*</note> is placed, the distribution is as follows: 
*   First, three points in the **Head** <term>Mastaka</term> 
*   Three points in the **Face** <term>Mukha*</term>
*   One each in the two **Shoulders** <term>Skandha</term>
*   One each in the pair of **Arms** <term>Bahu</term> 
*   One each in the two **Hands** <term>Kara</term> 
*   Five gems <note>likely representing five points of significance</note> in the **Heart** <term>Hridaya</term> 
*   One in the **Navel** <term>Nabha</term> 
*   One is to be assigned to the **Private Parts** <term>Guhya</term>
*   One in the **Knee** <term>Janu</term>
*   And one in the **Eye** <term>Netra</term>.

<meta>Continuing from the previous discussion on birth predictions and the influence of the Sun, this page details the "Human Diagram" (Naracakra), which maps planetary influences onto specific body parts to predict a person's fate. It then transitions into the concept of "Gandanta," the inauspicious "knot" or transitional points in the zodiac and lunar calendar.</meta>

<margin>
Jyotisha
9
</margin>

The remainder is known as the **Human Diagram** <term>Naracakra</term>, which is proclaimed for determining one's wandering and fate. If the influence falls upon the **feet**, a short life is produced; if upon the **knees**, there will be constant wandering.

If the influence is situated at the **genitals**, one is said to be devoted to others' wives <note>original: *paradararatto*; this reflects traditional social warnings found in medieval astrological texts</note>. At the **navel** and the **heart**, there is the attainment of all desired things and happiness. 

At the **fingertips**, one becomes a worker; from the **arms**, one becomes a supporter or lord. At the **shoulders**, there is strength or the consumption of intoxicating things <note>original: *skandhapani*, likely referring to the bearing of burdens or social status</note>. One who has the influence in the **mouth** becomes an eater of sweet foods <note>original: *mishtanna bhokta*; implying a life of luxury or refined tastes</note>, and at the **head**, one achieves a circular crown of success. 

Thus, regarding the movement of the stars: in the middle, in the east, or in the direction of the origin...

There exists a "junction-knot" <term>Gandanta</term> between the **Scorpion** <note>original: *Kita* or *Vrishchika*</note> and the **Horse** <note>original: *Haya* or *Dhanu*, referring to Sagittarius</note>, and similarly between the end of the **Bow** <note>Sagittarius</note> and the start of the **Scorpion**, or at the end of **Pisces** and the beginning of **Aries**. 

In all these transitional points, it is **inauspicious** for all undertakings. These "junction-knots" <term>Gandanta</term> are famously known to be avoided. Specifically, at the end of the lunar mansion **Revati** and the start of **Ashvini** <note>original: *Dastra*</note>, for a duration of two **time-units** <term>Muhurta</term>, one should avoid all auspicious works. 

In the middle of the **Ashlesha** constellation, for a duration of four **measures** <term>Nadi</term>, and similarly for those born under **Jyeshtha** or **Mula**, O best of kings! Know that the **Gandanta** is of three types: 

1. That of the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term>
2. That of the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>
3. That of the **Star** <term>Riksha</term>

This "junction-knot" is to be considered even from the moment of birth.

<meta>Continuing from the discussion on the Human Diagram and the transitional "junction-knots" (Gandanta) between zodiac signs, this page provides specific timing calculations for avoiding inauspicious periods, particularly the "Vishti" period (also known as Bhadra), and explains how its influence shifts between the three realms of existence—Heaven, the Underworld, and the Earth—based on the Moon's position.</meta>

A child should never be separated <note>original: *viyoge*; this may refer to specific ritual separations or birth conditions</note> from the influence of the girl <note>likely referring to the sign Virgo or a specific female energy</note>. The period known as **Vishti** <term>Vishti</term> <note>Also called Bhadra, this is one of the eleven 'Karanas' or half-lunar days in the Vedic calendar, generally considered inauspicious for starting new ventures</note> is regarded as a "vessel of blood" <note>original: *raktasya bhajanam*; a metaphor for its potentially violent or harmful nature</note>. 

At the **junction-knot** <term>Gandanta</term>, on the **tenth** and **eighth** lunar days, one should avoid the first five units of time <term>Ghatika</term> <note>One Ghatika is approximately 24 minutes</note>. On the **seventh** lunar day, at the end of twelve units of time, avoid three units. On the **third** lunar day, the influence is distant, occurring after twenty units of time.

The **"tail" of the Vishti period** <note>The final portion of an inauspicious period, sometimes considered less harmful than the beginning</note> ends on the lunar days dedicated to **Shiva** <note>The 14th lunar day or Chaturdashi</note> and on the **fourth** lunar day. If it occurs during two portions of the night or two portions of the day, then all tasks may be performed—so says the sage **Manu** <note>A legendary lawgiver and authority in Indian tradition</note>.

### The Residence of the Vishti Period

The location of this influence depends on the position of the Moon:

*   If the Moon resides in **Pisces**, **Aries**, **Scorpio**, or **Cancer**, the influence shines in the **Heavenly World** <term>Swargaloka</term>.
*   If the Moon is in the signs named **Virgo**, **Libra**, **Sagittarius**, or **Gemini**, it dwells in the **Serpent World** <term>Nagaloka</term> <note>The subterranean realm or underworld</note>.
*   If the Moon approaches **Leo**, **Aquarius**, **Taurus**, or **Capricorn**, it shines in the **Mortal World** <term>Mrityuloka</term> <note>The Earthly realm; the influence is only considered dangerous to humans when it resides here</note>.

**Bhadra** <note>Another name for the Vishti period</note>, the moonlight, or the "Daughter of the Sun" <note>original: *Dinakara-tanaya*; a name for the goddess Bhadra or the river Yamuna, but here referring to the astrological shadow</note> is not auspicious when traveling through the worlds. The **Vishti** period, the Sun, the **Inauspicious Alignment** <term>Vyatipata</term> <note>A specific astronomical yoga considered very malefic</note>, the **Constancy** <term>Dhrti</term> yoga, the **Birth Star**, and the **Cruel Stars** <note>Malefic Nakshatras</note> are all factors to be considered...

<page-num>21</page-num>
->**Astrology**<-

...leaving aside [what is] auspicious, thus the **Bhadra** <note>Also known as Vishti, an inauspicious period in the lunar calendar</note> occurs. In the months of **Ashadha**, **Ashvina**, and **Chaitra**, the "root" of the influence is in the **Moon** <note>original: *abje*; literally 'water-born', a common synonym for the Moon</note>. During the months of **Urja** <note>The month of Kartika (October–November)</note>, **Margashirsha**, **Jyeshtha**, and **Phalguna**, the root is in the **sky**. 

--- When it resides on **Earth** <term>Medini</term>, 
it will cause suffering to humans. 
In **Heaven**, it favors the **King**; 
in the **Underworld** <term>Patala</term>, it brings **wealth**. 

Regarding the **Mūla Nakshatra** <term>Mūla</term> <note>The 19th lunar mansion, associated with the 'Root' and the goddess of destruction, Nirriti</note>, the destruction caused by the root lasts for seven **Ghatikas** <note>approx. 2 hours and 48 minutes</note> from the start.

The results of the **"Mūla Tree"** <note>A divinatory system where different parts of the constellation's influence are mapped onto the parts of a tree</note> are as follows:
* If in the **Roots**: Destruction like fire.
* If in the **Trunk** <note>original: *vitapa*</note>: Loss of wealth and property.
* If in the **Branches** <note>associated with Rudra, the god of storms</note>: Destruction occurs.
* If in the **Leaves**: The person becomes famous and a doer of good deeds.
* If in the **Flowers**: One becomes a minister or counselor.
* If in the **Fruit**: Sovereignty <note>original: *sagare raja*; literally 'a king to the ocean'</note>, long life, and great power. 

This is the known fruit of the Mūla constellation.

### Divination by Calculation

Take the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term> combined with the **Watch** <term>Prahara</term> <note>A 3-hour period of the day or night</note>, the **Star** <note>Nakshatra</note>, and the **Weekday** <note>Vara</note>. Divide this sum by **seven**. The remainder indicates the result:

1. **One**: Fear of the government or King.
2. **Two**: Happiness and prosperity.
3. **Three**: A path toward financial gain.
4. **Four**: Village or domestic matters.
5. **Five**: Loss of a son or progeny.
6. **Six**: The arrival of disease.
<note>The text likely continues to seven/zero on the following page</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological divination. While the previous page focused on the "Bhadra" period and the "Mūla Tree," this page introduces the "Query Number" (Prichchanka) method and the "Planetary Body" (Grahapurusha) system, where planets are mapped onto human anatomy to predict the success of an inquiry.</meta>

<page-num>22</page-num>
->**Divination by Numbers and the Body**<-

One should recognize the signs of life and death, as well as the characteristics of the ninth and eighth houses. Regarding the **Query Number** <term>Prichchanka</term> <note>A number provided by a person seeking an astrological consultation, used as a mathematical base for the reading</note>: double the number, then add the value for the **Sun** and five for **Jupiter**, as taught in tradition. 

Add the **Lunar Day** <term>Tithi</term> and divide the sum by three. The remainder indicates the strength of the outcome. The **Sun** and **Mars** <note>original: *Angaraka*; the 'Glowing Ember'</note> are considered to be near each other [in this calculation]. When the triad of duty <note>original: *Dharma-traya*</note> is complete, **Jupiter**, the **Moon**, and **Saturn** are positioned. 

The direction, the weekday, the **Lunar Mansion** <term>Nakshatra</term>, and the **Lunar Day** are also factored in. From the remainder of the ascendant limb, one should declare the result:
* By **one** and **five**: Gain is attained.
* By **two** and **seven**: Likewise, success follows.
* **Six** and **four** are declared auspicious. 
* By [other numbers], loss is indicated. 

The **Deity of Fire** <term>Agni</term> spoke, and the **Ancestors** <term>Pitara</term> give sight. Whether by six and three, or by one and five, the placement of the planets on the body is as follows: 

* The **Sun** <term>Arka</term> is on the **head**. 
* The **Moon** is in the **mouth**. 
* **Mars** <term>Bhauma</term> is in the **heart**. 
* **Mercury** <term>Budha</term> is on the **waist**. 
* **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> should be placed on the [knees]. 
* **Saturn** <term>Shani</term> should be assigned to the **feet** and **toes**. 

Success is achieved at the end of the vessel <note>possibly referring to the completion of the ritual or calculation</note>; thus is the success of **Prajapati** <note>The Creator deity</note>. Conflict comes from the **Son of Earth** <note>original: *Bhaumiputra*; a common name for the planet Mars</note>, while **Mercury** <term>Saumya</term> <note>literally 'the gentle or moon-like one'</note> brings peace.

It shifts focus toward the influence of the Zodiac signs and Lunar Mansions on the success of specific tasks, such as buying, stockpiling, and lending money.</meta>

<page-num>१२</page-num>

<margin>Astrology 12</margin> When **Jupiter** <term>Jiva</term> and **Venus** <term>Sita</term> are prominent, there is essence and success; however, under **Saturn** <term>Manda</term>, the destruction of the work occurs. This is the established truth.

<margin>Signs</margin> In the signs of **Aries** <term>Aja</term>, **Taurus** <term>Vrisha</term>, and **Sagittarius** <term>Dhanvi</term>, there is a delay in the work. In **Leo** <note>original: *Hari*; literally "The Lion"</note>, and during the *Valava* period, there is obstruction. 
Success is attained in **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha</term>, while destruction and failure occur in **Capricorn** <term>Makara</term>, **Scorpio** <term>Vrischika</term>, and **Cancer** <term>Karka</term>. In **Gemini** <term>Mithuna</term> and **Virgo** <term>Kanya</term>—
<margin>Vision-Result</margin> —and in **Pisces** <term>Meena</term>, results come quickly. Such is the **omen** <term>Shakuna</term>. Under **Saturn**, the two types of buying should not be performed. <margin>No</margin> 
<margin>Day</margin> On **Wednesday** <term>Budha-vasara</term>, one may indeed make purchases. On **Tuesday** <term>Kuja</term>, one should perform the gathering or stockpiling of goods. 
<margin>Day</margin> Furthermore, if wealth is given under the **Lunar Mansions** <term>Nakshatra</term> of **Punarvasu**, **Vishakha**, **Ardra**, or **Chitra**, wealth will be acquired again without a doubt. Under **Bharani**, the three **"Prior" Mansions** <note>The "three Purvas": *Purva-Phalguni*, *Purva-Ashadha*, and *Purva-Bhadrapada*</note>, as well as **Ardra**, **Ashlesha**, **Magha**, **Chitra**, **Swati**, **Vishakha**, **Mula**, **Mrigashirsha**, and **Punarvasu**—whatever wealth is given or employed under these <margin>Know</margin>—
the wealth returns to the lender even if it seems to be running away from them. 

On **Tuesday** <term>Bhuvara</term>, on a **Joyous lunar day** <term>Nanda</term> <note>The 1st, 6th, or 11th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, and under the **Hand** mansion <term>Hasta</term>, [the results are calculated] by the numbers six <note>original: *rasa*</note>, ten <note>original: *dig*</note>, three <note>original: *vanni*</note>, and seven <note>original: *shaila*</note>. 
By the **Vedas** (4), the **Serpents** (8), **Shiva** (11), and the **Suns** (12), one calculates the timing of the **Moon**. <margin>Mars</margin>

and if *Purna* falls on a **Monday** <term>Soma</term>—these are known as **Days of Failure** <term>Asiddhi Tithis</term> for all undertakings.

Three signifies death; two signifies fear or distress; / 
one, five, and four indicate that nothing is certain.

### The Calculation of the Thorns of Time

The numerical codes <note>original: *Rama-dvi-pancha-rupa-rasa...*; a system of using words to represent numbers for calculation</note> for the days of the week are used to determine the **Thorns of Time** <term>Kantaka Kala-vela</term> <note>specific inauspicious periods within a day that "prick" or obstruct success</note>. These calculations for **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> and the other planets are based on the divisions of the day, beginning from sunrise. 

These periods, known as the **Thorns of Time** and the **Death-Bell** <term>Yamaghantaka</term>, are calculated starting from **Saturday** <term>Manda</term>, **Tuesday** <term>Bhauma</term>, and the other days. These specific times bring about various results: **Agitation** <term>Udvega</term>, **Illness** <term>Roga</term>, **Gain** <term>Labha</term>, **Inauspicious Speech**, and **Quarrels** <term>Kalaha</term>.

The duration of these periods for the **Sun** <term>Arka</term> and the other planets is **half a watch** <term>Yama-ardha</term> <note>A "watch" or *yama* is 3 hours; thus, these periods last 90 minutes</note>. 

### The Planetary Hours

The **Sun** <term>Arka</term> brings **Wrath** <note>original: *Krodha*</note>, followed by the periods of **Saturn** <term>Manda</term> and **Jupiter** <term>Jiva</term>. 

A **Planetary Hour** <term>Hora</term> is measured as **two and a half Ghatikas** <note>original: *Sardha-dvi-nadika*; 2.5 *nadikas* or *ghatikas* equals exactly 60 minutes</note>. These hours rotate repeatedly through the day. 

The effects of these hours <note>original: *Velas*</note> are as follows:
*   The hour of the **Sun** <term>Arka</term> causes distress and difficulty in travel. 
*   In the hour of the **Moon** <term>Soma</term>, one finds happiness with kinsmen and friends. 
*   The hours of the **Malefics** <note>referring here to Saturn or Mars</note> bring fear of fire and thieves. 
*   In the hour of **Mercury** <term>Budha</term>, one attains safety, health, and happiness. 
*   The hour of **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> brings the gain of wealth and granaries, though it may also lead to physical weariness <note>original: *vyadhi-prapti*; literally "attainment of ailment," likely referring to the exhaustion of labor or travel for gain</note>.

<summary>This page details the calculation and effects of the "Thorns of Time" (Kantaka) and "Death-Bell" (Yamaghantaka), which are specific inauspicious 90-minute intervals. It also defines the "Hora" as a 60-minute planetary hour and lists how each planet's hour influences daily activities.</summary>

...travel and difficulty. On **Saturday** <term>Manda</term>, if the day is chosen for a journey <term>Yatra</term>, it shall be auspicious. The stars and the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the event</note> become favorable when the **Moon** <term>Chandra</term> is joined with the Ascendant; this is certain to grant success.

For works involving stealth or departure, even the **Malefic planets** <term>Papa-graha</term> <note>planets like Saturn or Mars that usually indicate hardship</note> become givers of good fortune. **Mars** <term>Bhritatanaya</term> <note>literally "Son of the Earth"</note>...

This specific configuration is called the **Superior Alignment** <term>Adhi-yoga</term>. Truly, among all planetary combinations, the **Superior Alignment** is supreme. By the power of this alignment, kings conquer their enemies in battle. Through it, they achieve their departures and their returns; through it, they gain great fame and the **destruction of their enemies** <term>Ripu-vinasha</term>.

It establishes the auspicious months for starting construction and the directional orientations required based on the Sun's position in the zodiac.</meta>

**Page 14**
</margin>

Wealth, prosperity, growth, fear, and the attainment of fortune <note>original: *Lakshmi-prapti*</note>—these are the results for the one who commences the construction of a dwelling <term>Bhavana</term>, respectively, in the months starting from **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu calendar, roughly March-April</note>. According to the science of architecture <term>Vastu Shastra</term>, the results are determined by the position of the Sun.

When the **Sun** <term>Arka</term> resides in the signs of **Cancer** <term>Karki</term>, **Scorpio** <term>Vrishchika</term>, **Leo** <term>Hari</term>, or **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha</term>, then houses and other buildings should be constructed facing **East** or **West**.

If the Sun is in the signs of **Pisces** <term>Jhasha</term>, **Aries** <term>Mesha</term>, **Taurus** <term>Vrisha</term>, or **Scorpio** <note>The text repeats Scorpio, though traditional Vastu texts often list different signs for North/South orientation</note>, then one should make the dwellings face **South** or **North**. Otherwise, if one acts out of poor judgment, one suffers from disease, grief, fear, and loss.

The eight auspicious formulas <term>Aya</term> <note>mathematical calculations used to ensure a building's dimensions are in harmony with cosmic forces</note> are: **Banner** <term>Dhvaja</term>, **Bull** <term>Vrisha</term>, **Lion** <term>Simha</term>, and others in sequence. It is said that:

*   Under the **Banner** <term>Dhvaja</term> formula, death is conquered <note>or success is achieved</note>.
*   In the **Lion** <term>Simha</term>, there is victory.
*   In the **Dog** <term>Shvana</term>, there is disease of the eyes.
*   In the **Bull** <term>Vrisha</term>, one attains the power of a kingdom and great boons.
*   In the **Elephant** <term>Gaja</term>, there is happiness.
*   In the **Crow** <term>Vayasa</term>, all efforts are fruitless.

In the month of **Bhadrapada**, the head of the Spirit of the Land <term>Vastu-purusha</term> <note>the metaphorical deity or energy grid presiding over a building site</note> lies toward the **East**. In the three months beginning with **Margashira**, it lies in the **South**. In the month of **Phalguna**, it lies toward the **West**. In the three months beginning with **Jyeshtha**, it lies toward the **North**.

For the **Priestly class** <term>Vipra</term>, the **Banner** orientation facing East is prescribed. For **Kings** <term>Rajni</term>, the **Lion** orientation facing North...

### The Pot Diagram <term>Kalaśa Chakra</term>

When the **Sun** <term>Arka</term> is in the sign designated for house entry <term>Pravesha</term>, one must calculate the results using the Diagram of the Ritual Pot <term>Kalaśa Chakra</term>. The placement of the stars within this diagram determines the fortune or misfortune of the residents:

*   **One** star is placed at the **mouth** <note>original: *mukha*</note> of the pot.
*   **Three** stars are placed at the **neck** <note>original: *gale*</note>.
*   **Sixteen** stars are placed in the **sides/body** <note>original: *praachyādo*</note> in sequence.
*   **Four** stars are placed at the **front/top**.
*   **Three** stars are placed at the **base/anus** <note>original: *gude*</note>.

The results of these placements are as follows:
If the star falls in the mouth, there is a risk of "beheading" <note>likely a symbolic warning of great danger to the head of the household</note>. At the neck, there is a loss of wealth and a lack of stability. In the body of the pot, one finds **wealth and prosperity** <note>original: *dhanaṃ śrīśca*</note>, as well as the stability of Venus and happiness. However, if stars fall at the base, it indicates sorrow, miscarriage <note>original: *garbha-cyuti*</note>, or a state of poverty within the home.

### Auspicious Constellations for Fixed Works

The sages proclaim that **Rohini** and the **three Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> are known as the **Fixed** <term>Dhruva</term> stars. These are highly praised for:
1. Sowing seeds <term>Bija</term>.
2. Constructing palaces <term>Harmya</term> or cities.
3. Royal consecrations <term>Abhisheka</term>.
4. Planting gardens.
5. The first entry of a bride into the home.

Just as these are auspicious for a bride, they are praised for a **King's entry** <term>Nripa-pravesha</term> into a palace. However, entry is generally **not praised at night**; it should be performed during the day. 

There are **three types of entry** <term>Trividha Pravesha</term> mentioned in the tradition <note>These are traditionally Apurva (first entry to a new house), Sapura (entry after a journey), and Dwandwa (entry after renovation)</note>. 

The **first type** of entry is auspicious under the following stars: **Pushya**, the **Fixed stars** <term>Dhruva</term>, the **Soft stars** <term>Mridu</term>, **Swati**, **Hasta**, **Dhanishtha** <note>original: *Vasava*</note>, and **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>...

### The Mapping of the Lunar Mansions to the Zodiac Signs

*   **Aries** <note>original: *Mesha*</note> consists of the stars **Ashwini**, **Bharani**, and one quarter <note>original: *pada*</note> of **Krittika**.
*   **Taurus** <note>original: *Vrishabha*</note> consists of the remaining three quarters of **Krittika**, **Rohini**, and half of **Mrigashira**.
*   **Gemini** <note>original: *Mithuna*</note> consists of the other half of **Mrigashira**, <insert>Ardra</insert> <note>The star Ardra is implied here by the mathematical sequence</note>, and three quarters of **Punarvasu**.
*   **Cancer** <note>original: *Karkataka*</note> consists of the final quarter of **Punarvasu**, **Pushya**, and the end of **Ashlesha**.
*   **Leo** <note>original: *Simha*</note> consists of **Magha**, **Purva Phalguni**, and one quarter of **Uttara Phalguni**.
*   **Virgo** <note>original: *Kanya*</note> consists of three quarters of **Uttara Phalguni**, **Hasta**, and half of **Chitra**.
*   **Libra** <note>original: *Tula*</note> <note>The scribe implies Libra by the sequence</note> consists of the other half of **Chitra**, **Swati**, and three quarters of **Vishakha**. 2
*   **Scorpio** <note>original: *Vrishchika*</note> consists of the final quarter of **Vishakha**, **Anuradha**, and the end of **Jyeshtha**.
*   **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Dhanu*</note> consists of **Mula**, **Purva Ashadha**, and one quarter of **Uttara Ashadha**.
*   **Capricorn** <note>original: *Makara*</note> consists of three quarters of **Uttara Ashadha**, **Shravana**, and half of **Dhanishta**.
*   **Aquarius** <note>original: *Kumbha*</note> consists of the other half of **Dhanishta**, **Shatataraka**, and three quarters of **Purva Bhadrapada**.
*   **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note> consists of the final quarter of **Purva Bhadrapada**, **Uttara Bhadrapada**, and the end of **Revati**.

### The Effects of Saturn’s Entry

When Saturn enters these signs, the following results are observed:
In **Aries**, poverty departs <note>original: *dainyam-apaiti*</note>. In **Taurus**, one becomes prideful. <unclear>various afflictions</unclear> <note>original: *nānātti*</note>. In **Cancer**, one becomes wealthy. <unclear>the sound of Virgo</unclear> ...

### Animal Symbolism and Elemental Nature

In **Scorpio**, there is a certain impurity or darkness <note>original: *malinatta*</note>. In **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Chapa*</note>, the influence of the bow <term>Dhanus</term> prevails. In **Capricorn** <note>original: *Makara*</note>, the crocodile or sea-monster; in **Aquarius** <note>original: *Ghata*</note>, the water-pot; and in **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note>, the fish, whose face appears somewhat fearsome in the water.

The following creatures are assigned to the divisions:

3.  **Lion** <note>original: *Simha*</note>

7.  **Ram** <note>original: *Mesha*</note>

### Mathematical Formula for Predictions

To determine the specific outcome <term>Phala</term> of a transit or house placement:
Take the sum of the variables, **divide it by eight** <note>original: *ashta-bhakta*</note>, and by calculating the **remainder** <note>original: *shesha*</note>, the wise shall declare the result. 

### Durations of Planetary Transits

The duration of the stars <term>Nakshatra</term> and the movement through the signs are as follows:
*   **Saturn** <note>original: *Shani*</note> is known to stay for a long duration <note>Saturn typically spends 2.5 years in a sign, though the text mentions a 32-unit division here</note>.
*   **Rahu** and **Ketu** have their specific timings.
*   The **Moon** <note>original: *Chandrama*</note> stays in a single sign for **two days and a quarter** <note>original: *dina dvayam padam*</note>.

### Material and Social Associations

The five-fold groupings <term>Panchaka</term> starting from **Ashwini** relate to specific precious materials and classes of people. These include:
*   **Gold** <note>original: *Hema*</note>
*   **Jewels** <note>original: *Mani*</note>
*   **Pearls** <note>original: *Mukta*</note>
*   **Conch Shells** <note>original: *Shankha*</note>
*   **Clothing/Garments** <note>original: *Kanchu*</note>

Those of the **Priestly Class** <note>original: *Brahmana*</note> are associated with the influence of **Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note>. When the Moon is in an auspicious position regarding one's Duty <term>Dharma</term>, the results 

->become<-

beneficial, leading to the destruction of enemies and the attainment of **Gains** <term>Labha</term>.

<meta>This page continues the astrological discourse from the previous folio, transitioning from the mathematical durations of planetary transits to the specific material and spiritual outcomes—such as wealth, fame, and health—determined by the Sun’s entrance into a new zodiac sign and the influence of the lunar nodes.</meta>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>ASTRONOMY/ASTROLOGY</header>
<meta>catchword: Che</meta>

Success in endeavors, the acquisition of beautiful garments and the company of women, or the arrival of wealth and safety are predicted. One gains happiness, victory, fame, and great joy when the Sun <note>original: *Arki*</note> is favorably placed. 
Conversely, there may be a loss of intellect <note>original: *matti-labha-nishphala* - interpreted as "fruitless gain of mind"</note> or failure in assemblies. If **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon</note> is in the fifth house, it devours the results. In the absence of the Moon’s portion, the division is designated as **Shakuni**. 

In the second half, based on the calculation of fifteen <note>original: *dashardha*—ten plus half of ten</note>, the upper movements are governed by the **Naga** <term>Karana</term> <note>A Karana is half of a lunar day</note>. **Kinstughna** appears in the first half of the first lunar day <note>original: *pratipada*</note>. Then follow the **Chatushpada**, **Naga**, and **Kimstughna** intervals.
<margin>Regarding the Sun—</margin> When the **Sun** <note>original: *Ravi*</note> makes its transit <term>Sankranti</term>, it may occur during the intervals known as **Vishti**, **Bava**, or **Gara**. Or it may be situated in the intervals of **Vanija**, **Kinstughna**, or **Shakuni**.

When the Sun’s transit occurs while in the **Kaulava** interval, if the Sun is in an **upright** <note>original: *urdhva*</note> posture, it signifies the coming of disease and famine. If the Sun is **seated** <note>original: *upavishta*</note> or in an even position, then without a doubt, the people will obtain prosperity and happiness. If the transition is marked by a **terrible roar** <note>original: *ghoraravau*</note> or a sound like a distant echo, it signifies movement and change.

The transit is named **Mahodari** if it occurs on a Tuesday <note>original: *Are*</note>. It is called **Mandakini** on a Wednesday <note>original: *Jne*</note>, and it is named **Manda** on a Thursday <note>original: *Guro*</note>.

# The Appearance and Ritual Requirements of the Transit

The one who is as dark as crushed eye-paint <note>original: *bhinna-anjana*—referring to the deep black color of collyrium</note> and has white tusks, holding a wheel, again strikes and causes the subjects to yield. The Big-Bellied one <note>original: *mahodari*—likely a personification of the transit deity or a specific goddess</note> is enough to terrify the thieves; those who have small eyes are rushing. And furthermore, the Mandarava flowers and the groups of noble priests <note>original: *dvijavaragana*</note> are not engaged in other business.
<margin>and</margin> The howling of the jackals and the sounds made in the night are heard; in the forenoon, they burn at the end of the time for the best of the priests. In the midday, and especially at night, <margin>Shu</margin>
<margin>Shra</margin> twice at sunset, in the evening, and for the night-wanderers <note>spirits or demons</note> at midnight. Then, at the end of the night, and at the time of dawn, for the cowherds <margin>Pra</margin> there should be offerings of food and sweet milk pudding <term>payasam</term>, along with five-fold edible offerings of milk, curd, colored rice, jaggery, honey, clarified butter, and sugar.

As appropriate to the sequence, one should offer white cloth, red variegated cloth, yellow cloth, blue cloth, blankets, skins, black garments, bark-cloth <note>original: *valkala*—traditional ascetic clothing made of tree bark</note>, and gold. For the consideration of the lunar day, one should use rubies, sapphires, silver, diamonds, hessonite <term>gomedaka</term>, coral, topaz, neck-ornaments, pearls, and crystal.
