Regarding the calculation of numbers: multiply the given number by three and divide the sum by eight. Experts say that if the remainder is even, one result occurs, and if odd, another. At the time of a query <term>Prashna</term> <note>a branch of astrology where a chart is cast to answer a specific question</note>, the wise should observe the following: multiply the rising sign <term>Lagna</term> <note>the zodiac sign appearing on the eastern horizon at the moment of the query</note> by four <note>original: *veda*—the number four, symbolizing the four Vedas</note> and add the current time measured in <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>a unit of time approximately equal to 24 minutes</note>. 

If the rising sign is a **Moveable** sign <note>original: *Chara*—Aries, Cancer, Libra, or Capricorn</note>, add five (5). If it is a **Fixed** sign <note>original: *Sthira*—Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, or Aquarius</note>, add ten (10). If it is a **Dual** sign <note>original: *Dvisvabhava*—Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, or Pisces</note>, add fifteen (15). Divide this total; the remainder is then combined with the current lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>.

---

Measure the shadow of the feet <note>a traditional method of determining the time of day by measuring the length of one's own shadow in "foot-lengths"</note> and add it to the calculation; divide the total by one hundred and twenty-one. The quotient should be understood as the <term>Ghatikas</term>, and the remainder indicates the <term>Kalas</term> <note>a smaller unit of time, a fraction of a Ghatika</note>.

Now, regarding the directions for travel <note>original: *gamana*</note>: 
In the **East**, the constellations are Jyeshtha and Dhanishtha. 
In the **South**, they are Shravana and Ashvini. 
In the **West**, they are Rohini and Pushya. 
In the **North**, they are the "Uttara" stars <note>referring to Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, or Uttara Bhadrapada</note> and Hasta. 

On a **Full** <term>Purna</term> lunar day <note>the 5th, 10th, and 15th days of the lunar fortnight</note>, the "spike" <note>original: *shula*—an inauspicious direction that causes "pain" or failure</note> is in the East. 
On an **Empty** <term>Rikta</term> day <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days</note>, it is in the South. 
On a **Victorious** <term>Jaya</term> day <note>the 3rd, 8th, and 13th days</note>, it is in the West. 
On a **Joyful** <term>Nanda</term> day <note>the 1st, 6th, and 11th days</note>, it is in the North. 

If one travels in these forbidden directions on these specific days, their life and purpose will be destroyed. However, one is released from these faults during the transit of Jupiter, during marriages, or in specific types of journeys.

# Astrological Timing and Travel Omens

Regarding wisdom and knowledge, one should act on **Wednesday** <note>original: *Budha*</note>; for all general tasks, **Monday** <note>original: *Chandra*</note>; for acts of humility or discipline, **Saturday** <note>original: *Manda*</note>; and for service to the King or government, **Sunday** <note>original: *Ravi*</note>. For matters concerning illness and war, **Tuesday** is noted. These combinations are known as the **Formidable Conjunctions** <term>Ghora Yoga</term> <note>intense or potentially harsh astrological timings that require specific focus</note>.

The following are the specific associations between the Zodiac Signs <term>Rashi</term> and the Lunar Mansions <term>Nakshatra</term>:
- In **Aries** <note>original: *Mesha*</note>, the mansion is **Magha**.
- In **Taurus** <note>original: *Vrishabha*</note>, it is the fifth [tithi] and **Hasta**.
- In **Gemini** <note>original: *Mithuna*</note>, the ninth [tithi] is the master.
- In **Cancer** <note>original: *Karka*</note>, it is the sixth and **Anuradha**.
- In **Leo** <note>original: *Simha*</note>, the third and **Mula** are spoken of.
- In **Virgo** <note>original: *Kanya*</note>, it is the tenth and **Shravana**.
- In **Libra** <note>original: *Tula*</note>, it is the third and **Shatabhisha**.
- In **Scorpio** <note>original: *Vrishchika*</note>, it is the seventh and **Revati**.
- In **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Dhanu*</note>, it is **Bharani** and the four <note>original: *Veda*—the number four, symbolizing the four Vedas</note>.
- In **Capricorn** <note>original: *Makara*</note>, it is the eighth and **Rohini**.
- In **Aquarius** <note>original: *Kumbha*</note>, it is the eleventh and eighth.
- In **Pisces** <note>original: *Mina*</note>, it is the twelfth and **Pushya**.

---

A journey <term>Yatra</term> shall be successful and auspicious if undertaken during the mansions of **Anuradha** <note>original: *Mitra-datta*</note>, **Shravana**, or **Hasta**, or under the solar mansions <note>original: *Aditya-nakshatra*</note>. Success is also granted by the stars **Chitra** and **Vishakha**. However, one should avoid traveling when the stars of Indra are present, as this brings fear of the "umbrella" <note>symbolizing royal or state-sanctioned punishment/interference</note>.

Regarding directions:
When traveling to the **West**, certain omens prevail. From the **East**, the Sun <note>original: *Divakara*</note> influences the path. # Auspicious Travel and the Gateways of the Directions

The divisions arising from the constellations <note>original: *Kaskibhava*</note> and the movements of the planets are explained through the twenty parts of the solar and lunar cycles. From the six transitions <note>original: *Shat-shakranti*</note>, the lunar days <term>Tithi</term>, and the sacred festivals, one determines the ten positions of the "Yama" <note>the god of death, here referring to inauspicious time-lords</note> and the three-fold steps of the path.

The gateways of the directions <term>Dig-dvara</term> are governed by the South <note>original: *Dakshina*</note> and the West <note>original: *Varuna*</note>. For those following the lines of the road, the Southeast <note>original: *Agni-dik*</note> is established by the sequence of seven-seven-three <note>a mathematical calculation for determining planetary strength</note>. 

In this matter, one should not ignore the position of **Mercury** <note>original: *Budha*</note> when planning a journey. One must not go toward the **North** <note>original: *Uttara-asha*</note> when the ten-part divisions are unfavorable. Likewise, one should not travel to the **West** <note>original: *Pratichi*</note> under the influence of the constellations **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitra*</note> or **Ashvini**.

### Universal Travel and Planetary Gaze

However, when the constellation **Hasta** is aligned with **Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note>, one may travel in **all directions** <note>original: *Sarva-dik-prayayana*</note> with success. On the first day of travel, the three-fold fixed stars provide stability. Travel is considered inauspicious in the middle of certain mansions, or during the "sharp" <term>Tikshna</term> periods. In the latter parts of the light constellations, the stars are divided into three parts; travel is forbidden in the middle or final portions of these thirty divisions.

The movement of the Sun <note>original: *Kararkanti*</note> and the grace of Jupiter <note>original: *Ijya*</note> always bring auspiciousness. However, one must consider the **Face of the Direction** <term>Dig-mukha</term>. 

One should never travel if the following are positioned "at the forehead" <note>original: *Lalate*—meaning directly in front of the traveler's path</note>:
- The **Sun** <note>original: *Ravi*</note>
- **Venus** <note>original: *Shukra*</note>
- **Mars** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>
- **Rahu** <note>original: *Tamas*—the shadow planet</note>
- The **Moon** <note>original: *Soma*</note>
- **Mercury** <note>original: *Saumya*</note>
- **Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note>

To travel toward the "gaze" of these planets brings hardship. Special care is noted for the constellation **Pushya** <note>original: *Tishya*</note>...

# The Influence of Venus and the Houses of the Sun

When the Moon is in the constellation of **Abhijit** <note>original: *Abhijit*; the "Victorious" 28th intercalary constellation</note> or in the sign of **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note>, and if one travels when **Venus** <note>original: *Shukra*</note> and the Moon are in specific alignment, the journey is fruitful. For those desiring sons, the journey should be undertaken when the planets are positioned in the forward houses; otherwise, they should not set out.

Traveling directly toward the direction of **Venus** <term>Prati-shukra</term> <note>literally "Facing Venus"; an astrological prohibition against traveling in the direction where Venus is currently visible, as it is thought to "blind" or block the traveler's success</note> is inauspicious. This rule applies particularly to those born of the lineages of Vatsa, Bhargava, and Bharadvaja. In times of local upheaval <note>original: *vishaya-plave*</note>, or for the purpose of sprinkling <note>consecration</note>, or for **marriages** and the use of new **vehicles**, one must carefully calculate the planetary strengths.

<margin>Destruction</margin> Even when traveling between a single city or village, a favorable alignment of the gentle planets brings a successful journey. **Venus** is particularly auspicious in certain positions. Furthermore, the **Moon** <note>original: *Chandra*</note> is considered a giver of auspicious results when situated in the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of departure</note> at the time of travel.

### Planetary Obstacles and Remedies

Malefic planets <note>original: *Papa-graha*</note> generally cause the destruction of the work at hand. However, they may grant auspicious results if they are situated in the "own-foot" divisions. If a planet is ill-placed in the house of action, it is not considered auspicious. Before departing, one must meditate upon the **Lord of the Direction** <note>original: *Dig-isha*; the specific deity presiding over the direction of travel</note>; otherwise, the journey may cause the **destruction** <note>original: *Nasha*</note> of the traveler's home or place of birth.

The **Sun** <note>original: *Dinakara*; literally "the day-maker"</note> creates **fear** <note>original: *Bhayam*</note> when placed in the second house from the traveler's natal sign. However, when the Sun is in the **third house** <term>Duschikya</term> <note>a technical term derived from the Greek "dystychia," used in ancient Indian astrology for the third house</note>, it expands one's dear desires and brings success. <unclear>Shambhu</unclear>...

<margin>Astrology</margin>The Sun <note>original: *Divakara*; literally "the day-maker"</note> situated in the third and fourth <term>Hibuka</term> <note>a Sanskritized Greek term *hypogeion*, referring to the 4th house or the "lower midheaven"</note> houses bestows honor, yet also brings poverty. When the Sun is located in the sixth house <note>the house of obstacles</note>, it becomes a destroyer of enemies. In the seventh house, it causes affliction to the luster of the body. Situated in the ninth <term>Dharma</term> <note>the house of luck and religion</note> house, the Sun generates success in one's actions; in the tenth <term>Karma</term> house, it leads to the gain of wealth. Residing in the eleventh house, it brings the sight of riches, but when the Sun enters the twelfth <term>Vyaya</term> <note>the house of expenditure and loss</note> house, it brings great calamity. 1

In the third house <term>Sahajabhavana</term> <note>the house of siblings and courage</note>, it causes stomach ailments. In the fourth house, it leads to the failure of one's work. In the sixth house, it speaks of gaining wealth, yet for that person, there is a risk of untimely death <term>Upamrityu</term>. 2

The Moon <note>original: *Shashi*</note> in the ninth house creates fear. When the Moon occupies the tenth house, it brings various kinds of great happiness. In the eleventh house, it creates gains, while in the twelfth house, it causes the loss of one's own wealth. 2

Saturn <note>original: *Shaurani*; a name for Shani, the son of the Sun</note> in the first house causes sorrow. In the [sixth] house, it grants victory over enemies. If Saturn is situated in the fourth house <term>Patala</term> <note>literally "the underworld," referring to the bottom-most point of the astrological chart</note>, it causes fear of enemies.

One should perform the entry into the home with these rites.

In the months of **Karttika** <note>October/November</note> and **Pausha**...

### Now, the Compatibility Factors <note>original: *Atha Kūṭa*</note>

The factors for matching are: **Social Class** <note>original: *Varṇa*</note>, **Innate Control** <note>original: *Vaśya*</note>, **Star-energy** <note>original: *Tārā*</note>, **Instinctive Nature** <note>original: *Yoni*</note>, **Planetary Friendship** <note>original: *Graha-maitri*</note>, and <margin>Pulse <note>original: *Nāḍī*</note></margin> **Temperamental Grouping** <note>original: *Gaṇa-maitrī*</note>. These factors are considered increasingly powerful in the order they are listed.

Regarding the Social Classes of the Zodiac:
**Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces** are the **Priests** <note>original: *Vipras*</note>. 
**Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius** are the **Kings** <note>original: *Bhūpās*</note>. 
**Capricorn, Taurus, and Virgo** are the **Merchants** <note>original: *Vaiśyas*</note>. 
**Libra, Aquarius, and Gemini** are the **Laborers** <note>original: *Śūdras*</note>.

Friendship is described based on these. Regarding the **Control** <term>Vaśya</term> factor: human signs are generally compatible with and controllable by other humans, except for the sign of **Leo**. Some authorities state that, with the exception of **Scorpio**, a woman is controllable by a man and vice-versa. **Scorpio, Pisces, and Cancer** are the **Watery signs**; all the remaining signs are known as "waterless" or dry signs.

The auspiciousness of the star-energy <term>Tārā</term> is determined by the positions; certain odd-numbered positions are considered malefic, while others are beneficial for well-being. 3 Regarding the **Instinctive Nature** <term>Yoni</term> <note>literally "source" or "womb," referring to the animal spirit associated with a birth star</note>, the classifications are: **Horse, Elephant, Ram, Snake, Cat, Rat, Buffalo, Tiger, Deer, Dog, Monkey, Mongoose, Bull, and Lion.** This sequence represents the various animal instincts assigned to the stars.

The following pairs represent **Natural Enmity** <note>original: *vaira*</note> and should be avoided: 
The **Cow and Tiger**, the **Elephant and Lion**, the **Horse and Buffalo**, the **Rat and Mongoose**, the **Snake and Cat**, and the **Monkey and Ram**. These pairings represent innate conflict. Knowing this, those who desire a prosperous and happy life should exert great effort to avoid such matches between a couple <note>original: *dampati*</note> during the marriage selection process. 4

### Planetary Rulerships <note>The foundation for Planetary Friendship</note>

**Mars** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note> is the ruler of **Aries and Scorpio**. 
**Venus** <note>original: *Śukra*</note> is the ruler of **Taurus and Libra**. 
**Mercury** <note>original: *Saumya*</note> is the ruler of **Gemini and Virgo**. 
The **Moon** <note>original: *Śaśī*</note> is the ruler of **Cancer**. 
**Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note> is the ruler of **Pisces and Sagittarius**.

**Jupiter** and **Saturn** are the rulers of **Capricorn** and **Aquarius**. The **Sun** is the ruler of **Leo**. As stated by the masters of calculation <note>original: *gaṇitajñaiḥ*, referring to those skilled in astronomy and mathematical astrology</note>, **Mars, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, the Sun, Jupiter, and Saturn**—these are the lords of the zodiac signs. 

Between the **Sun** <note>original: *Arka*</note> and **Saturn** <note>original: *Manda*</note> there is always **Enmity** <note>original: *vaira*</note>. Between **Mars** <note>original: *Kuja*</note> and **Saturn** it is the same. **Jupiter** <note>original: *Guru*</note> and **Venus** <note>original: *Śukra*</note> are always in enmity, as are **Mercury** <note>original: *Budha*</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: *Soma*</note>. **Mars** <note>original: *Bhūmija*</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: *Indu*</note> are always in conflict, and **Venus** <note>original: *Bhārgava*</note> and the **Sun** <note>original: *Arka*</note> are constantly so. These **Natural Enmities** <term>Nitya-Vaira</term> should be avoided with great effort. 

Regarding the **Sun** <note>original: *Ravi*</note>: **Saturn** and **Venus** are his enemies. **Mercury** <note>original: *Śaśija*, literally "born of the Moon"</note> is neutral. The remaining planets <note>Moon, Mars, and Jupiter</note> are his friends. 

For the **Moon** <note>original: *Śītaru*, "the cold-rayed one"</note>: The **Sun** <note>original: *Tīvrāmśu*, "the sharp-rayed one"</note> and **Mercury** <note>original: *Himaraśmija*</note> are friends. The remaining planets are neutral. 

For **Mars** <note>original: *Kuja*</note>: **Jupiter** <note>original: *Jīva*</note>, the **Moon** <note>original: *Indu*</note>, and the **Sun** are friends. **Mercury** <note>original: *Jña*</note> is the enemy. **Venus** <note>original: *Sita*</note> and **Saturn** <note>original: *Arkī*, "son of the Sun"</note> are neutral. 

For **Mercury** <note>original: *Budha*</note>: The **Sun** and **Venus** are friends. The **Moon** <note>original: *Himagu*</note> is the enemy. The others are neutral. 

For **Jupiter** <note>original: *Suraguru*, "teacher of the gods"</note>: The **Sun** <note>original: *Saumya* is an error in the OCR; it usually refers to Mercury, but the context here for Jupiter's friends is Sun, Moon, and Mars</note> and the **Moon** are friends. **Saturn** is neutral. **Venus** <note>original: *Śukra*</note> and **Mercury** are enemies.

These relationships determine the effects of the planetary periods <term>Daśā</term> and their specific transits through the houses of the birth chart. 

### The Three Temperaments <term>Gaṇas</term>

The stars are categorized into three spiritual temperaments <term>Gaṇas</term> which define a person's nature and their compatibility with others. 

**The Divine Temperament** <term>Deva-gaṇa</term>
The lunar mansions of **Ashwini, Mrigashira, Hasta, Swati, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha**, and **Revati** are known as the **Divine group**. Those born under these stars are said to possess a refined, virtuous, and peaceful nature.

**The Human Temperament** <term>Manuṣya-gaṇa</term>
**Bharani, Ardra, Rohini**, and the three "Latter" stars <note>original: *uttarāṇāṃ trayeva*, referring to Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> are known as the **Human group**. This group represents a mix of qualities, balancing earthly desires with spiritual potential.

**The Demonic Temperament** <term>Rākṣasa-gaṇa</term>
The mansions of **Krttika** <note>represented in the text by its presiding deity, <term>Agni</term></note>, **Magha, Vishakha, Ashlesha, Shatabhisha, Chitra, Dhanishta, Jyeshtha**, and **Mula** are categorized as the **Demonic group**. 
<note>In Indian astrology, "Demonic" does not imply evil, but rather a forceful, strong-willed, and intense temperament.</note>

### Compatibility of Temperaments

When matching individuals for marriage or partnership, the following rules of affection <note>original: *prītti*</note> apply:

*   **Excellent:** Compatibility is highest when both partners belong to the **same group** <note>original: *svakule*, literally "one's own family/class"</note>.
*   **Moderate:** The union between the **Divine** and **Human** temperaments is considered average or fair.
*   **Conflict:** A union between the **Divine** and **Demonic** temperaments leads to constant **Quarrels** <note>original: *kalaha*</note>.
*   **Severe Misfortune:** A union between the **Human** and **Demonic** temperaments results in **Death** <note>original: *mṛtyu*; this often symbolizes the metaphorical "death" of the relationship or extreme hardship rather than literal passing</note>.

In the calculation of planetary periods <term>Daśā</term>, placement in the **twelfth house** brings poverty, while placement in the **third** or **eleventh houses** may cause varying degrees of suffering or sound <note>original: *svara*, potentially referring to the resonance or vibrational quality of the planet's influence</note>.

<summary>This page classifies the twenty-seven Nakshatras into three spiritual temperaments—Divine, Human, and Demonic—and outlines the compatibility results when these temperaments interact in a marriage match.</summary>

->**The Positions of the Zodiac Signs**<-

In a **sixth-eighth** placement <term>ṣaḍaṣṭaka</term>, there may be death, while longevity is promised by the eighth-sixth. All these factors must be carefully grasped and considered during a marriage <term>vivāha</term>. This includes the signs of **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha</term>, **Cancer** <term>Karka</term>, **Sagittarius** <term>Cāpa</term>, the signs of **Mercury** <note>Gemini and Virgo</note>, **Aquarius** <term>Ghaṭa</term>, **Capricorn** <term>Mṛga</term>, or **Leo** <term>Siṃha</term>.

Between **Pisces** <term>Mīna</term> and **Scorpio** <term>Ali/Kīṭa</term>, or when **Aquarius** and **Gemini** are joined, or **Capricorn** with **Virgo** <term>Kanyā</term>, one should not form a **ninth-fifth** relationship. ### The Three Temperaments of the Stars

The stars are divided into three "natures" or temperaments <term>gaṇa</term> that define a person's character:

1. **The Divine Nature** <term>deva-gaṇa</term>:
The stars **Ashwini**, **Pushya**, **Mrigashira** <note>original: *saumya*</note>, **Hasta**, **Punarvasu**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *maitra*</note>, **Shravana**, and **Revati** belong to the gods. These are considered peaceful and noble temperaments.

2. **The Human Nature** <term>mānuṣa-gaṇa</term>:
The **three Uttara stars** <note>Uttaraphalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Rohini**, **Bharani**, and **Ardra**—these nine stars <note>counting the Uttaras and associated stars</note> are of the human temperament. These represent a blend of qualities.

3. **The Demonic Nature** <term>rākṣasa-gaṇa</term>:
The stars **Krittika**, **Vishakha**, **Magha**, **Ashlesha**, **Shatabhishaj**, and **Chitra**... <note>the text breaks off here</note> belong to the demonic temperament. This indicates a more intense, assertive, or volatile nature.

# Page 28

<margin>Astrology 28</margin> Then, once **Planetary Friendship** <term>Graha Maitri</term> is established, one considers **Sign Compatibility** <term>Rashi Kuta</term>. In the second position is **Star Compatibility** <term>Tara Kuta</term>, and in the third is **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi Kuta</term>. In this current Age of Darkness <note>original: *Kali Yuga*; the current epoch in Hindu cosmology</note>, the scholar Hemadri states in the *Brahma-Gaya* that these metrics are of special importance.

**Temperament Compatibility** <term>Gana Kuta</term> is especially praised, and should be calculated from the first letter of the name through to the vowel. **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi Kuta</term> is praised when calculated from the lines <note>original: *rekha*; likely referring to the lines of the hand or the birth chart</note> to the end, while **Sign Compatibility** <term>Rashi Kuta</term> follows the sequence from the initial name to the fixed sign. The bride’s sign is declared and the groom’s sign is determined; one must understand the compatibility between these two to ensure **mutual attraction** <term>vashyatva</term> and harmony.

According to the teachings of the sage Parashara <note>A foundational figure in Indian astrology and author of the *Brihat Parashara Hora Shastra*</note>, one should not proceed with a marriage if the planetary lords of the couple are not friendly. If they are mutual enemies, it leads to a diminished or difficult life.

Furthermore, the friendship between the lords of the divisional charts <term>amsha-mitra</term> <note>Specifically the *Navamsha* or ninth-division chart, which reveals the internal strength and fruitfulness of a marriage</note> is necessary for the blessing of children and the couple's mutual harmony. In the *Varaha Samhita* <note>The "Great Compilation" by the 6th-century astronomer Varahamihira</note>, it is noted that without such friendship between these planetary lords, the couple will eventually suffer separation.

Therefore, this assessment must be performed for the sake of the couple, as it determines the blessing of sons. In the cases of signs such as **Pisces** and **Taurus**, even if certain conditions vary, the marriage of the couple should be performed <note>These signs are often considered to have special exemptions from certain compatibility faults</note>.

# Page 28

<margin>Marriage Compatibility</margin> The sage Mandavya states that for the blessing of **progeny and grandchildren** <note>original: *putrapautrakam*</note>, different Vedic traditions emphasize different harmonies. For the followers of the Rigveda <note>original: *Bahvricha*</note>, mutual friendship is considered the highest factor. According to the Yajurveda, **Star Compatibility** <term>Tarika</term> and **Temperament Compatibility** <term>Gana Kuta</term> are paramount. Meanwhile, for those of the Atharvaveda, **Sign Compatibility** <term>Rashi</term> is the primary measure of success.

The sage Sharanga explains that concerning **Species Compatibility** <term>Yoni</term>, **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi</term>, and the various temperaments, even if <unclear>bhrushru</unclear> certain auspicious signs are missing, if there is **Planetary Friendship** <term>Kheta Maitri</term> between the lords of the birth charts, the union will bring favorable results. 

Even in cases where there is no **Star Compatibility** <term>Tara</term> or if there is a "break" in **Species Compatibility** <term>Yoni Bhanga</term>, the marriage may still be auspicious if the **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi</term> is sound or if the "fault of the dog" <note>original: *shva-dosha*; a specific astrological affliction or incompatibility</note> is resolved. Such a union is declared auspicious by the greatest of sages.

### Harmonious Sign Relationships

Specifically, the sages highlight the following pairs:
*   **Capricorn** <term>Makara</term> and **Cancer** <term>Karkata</term>
*   **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha</term> and **Leo** <term>Simha</term>

These pairs sit in a **mutual seventh-house relationship** <note>original: *samasaptaka*; signs located directly opposite each other, which can create a balancing "mirror" effect</note>. Furthermore, signs in a **fifth-ninth relationship** <note>original: *panchama-navama*; a trine relationship considered naturally harmonious in Vedic astrology</note> provide great strength to the marriage. A marriage conducted with these planetary strengths ensures the birth of children and grandchildren.

The wise scholars consider **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi Kuta</term> to be as vital as the **sacred wedding thread** <note>original: *kanya-kantha-sutra*; the necklace symbolizing the marital bond</note> worn by the bride. For this reason, one must carefully avoid the "middle" pulse conflict <note>original: *madhya-varja*; one of the three types of Nadi energy that, if shared by both partners, is said to cause health issues</note> in all countries and regions...

<summary>This page outlines the specific astrological metrics (Kutas) prioritized by different Vedic lineages and lists favorable zodiac pairings, such as Capricorn-Cancer, that ensure a successful marriage and progeny.</summary>

<keywords>Mandavya, Nadi Kuta, Gana Kuta, Rashi, Progeny, Samasaptaka, Marriage Astrology, Vedic Traditions</keywords>

# Page 30

<margin>Astrology</margin>

The **Two-fold Compatibility** <term>Dvi-kuta</term> is like the necklace and earrings of the bride. The **Three-sign Compatibility** <term>Trairashi-kuta</term> should also be performed. If there is mutual friendship, it does not cause harm. In the **Two-fold Compatibility**, the woman’s birth sign <margin>Rama</margin> should be considered primary. It is said that in various regions <note>original: *deshe deshe*</note>, the **Five-fold Compatibility** <term>Pancha-kuta</term> is prescribed for marriage. In the application of the Five-fold Compatibility <margin>self</margin>, even if the signs are different, if there is **Single Lordship** <note>original: *ekadhipatya*; when both signs are ruled by the same planet</note>, or if the lords are mutual friends, it is considered auspicious.

In the auspicious marriage chamber, whether in the regions of **Malwa** <note>Central India</note>, **Gauda** <note>Bengal</note>, **Panchala** <note>Punjab/North India</note>, or **Gujarat**, the **Species Compatibility** <term>Yoni</term> is analyzed. In **Saurashtra** and other lands, if there is hostility between the planets <note>original: *graha-vairam*</note>, then neither the **Class Compatibility** <term>Vaishya-kuta</term> nor the **Species Compatibility** <term>Yoni-kuta</term>, nor the **Human Compatibility** <note>original: *nara*</note> will suffice to save the union. ———————

————— However, if there is friendship among the planetary lords, the marriage brings good fortune. For those born under the **Mula** constellation and others, if they are free of faults at the time of marriage, the sacred thread is tied. If the **Life-force** <note>original: *Jiva*; often referring to the planet Jupiter or the vital breath</note> is destroyed, does the fault not remain? Can a **burnt seed** ever produce a sprout? <note>A classic philosophical metaphor: just as a seed scorched by fire cannot grow, an astrological union lacking vital planetary strength cannot flourish.</note>

<summary>This page discusses the regional variations in marriage compatibility (Kuta) across India and emphasizes that planetary friendship can override other minor astrological faults.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of marriage compatibility metrics (Kutas). While the previous page discussed regional variations and the Five-fold Compatibility (Pancha-kuta), this page focuses on specific exceptions where certain negative alignments are overlooked and details the influence of specific constellations—particularly Mula, Ardra, and Vishakha—on the health and progeny of the couple, citing the authority of the sage Garga.</meta>

->**Auspiciousness**<-

Neither the **Human-Sign Compatibility** <term>Nara-rashi-kuta</term>, nor the **Temperament Compatibility** <term>Gana</term>, nor the **Species Compatibility** <term>Yoni</term> are required if the **Distance of the Woman's Constellation** <term>Stri-dirgha</term> rule is excluded <note>The Stri-dirgha rule ensures the groom’s constellation is sufficiently distant from the bride’s to ensure longevity</note>; otherwise, the Creator <note>original: *Vedhas*</note> provides for the **Mahendra** connection <note>A specific favorable distance between stars</note>. The associations of the 6th and 10th positions are auspicious, as is marriage to the **daughter of a maternal uncle** <note>original: *matula-putrika*; this practice is accepted in certain South Indian Brahmin traditions but often debated in Northern texts</note>. If a man is born under the constellations of **Mula**, **Ardra**, or **Vishakha**, the **Mahendra** influence is produced; such a man causes no fault in the marriage.

One must avoid conditions leading to **female illness** <note>original: *stri-rogam*</note> in all circumstances. It is said that birth in the final quarter <term>pada</term> of the **Mula** constellation destroys one's own son. If a child is born in that quarter, they are neither killed nor truly afflicted by disease <note>original: *na chaiva hato rugaye*; implying a spiritual or astrological affliction rather than a physical one</note>. Regarding **Vishakha** and **Jyeshtha**, birth in the first quarter is auspicious, while the final quarter is not favorable for the groom. Regarding the **Pulse Compatibility** <term>Nadi</term> for the twice-born <note>original: *dvijamanva*; the upper castes</note>, and for the merchant class <term>Vaishya</term>...

...then the **Sign Compatibility** <term>Rashi-kuta</term> is analyzed according to the rules of **Class** <term>Varna</term> and **Species** <term>Yoni</term>. Even if the signs are in the 3rd and 11th positions, or the 4th and 10th positions, one may proceed without **Planetary Friendship** <term>Graha-maitri</term>, nor is it required in the mutual 7th position. If both are in the **same sign**, the marriage is auspicious; if they share the same divisional sign <term>bhanshe</term>, it is also considered to provide good fortune. If the constellations are auspicious, the sage **Darunaka** has said as much. For the signs of **Sagittarius** <term>Dhanu</term>, **Pisces** <term>Meena</term>, and **Cancer** <term>Kulira</term>, which are ruled by Jupiter <note>original: *jivijo*</note>, the end of life or death-like suffering is avoided. This is the weight of the word of **Sage Garga**, regarding those with afflicted yogas...

<page-num>31</page-num>
<header>Astro.</header>

->**Auspiciousness in Rituals**<-

If the Moon is in an odd-numbered position <note>1st, 3rd, 5th, etc.</note> counting from the Sun's constellation, including the intercalary star **Abhijit**, one should not discard such times for auspicious works <note>original: *na varjayet shubha-karmasu*; implying that certain lunar-solar frictions are permissible if other factors are strong</note>. The **Sun** <term>Ravi</term> is favorable when situated in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses from the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>. 

Similarly, **Mars** <term>Kuja</term> and **Saturn** <term>Arka-putra</term>, when placed in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, and the **Moon** <term>Shashi</term> in the 3rd, 6th, 7th, 10th, or 11th houses, bestow great auspiciousness during the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Vratabandha</term>, **Marriage** <term>Vivaha</term>, the **Tonsure Rite** <term>Chaula</term>, **Sacrifices** <term>Yajna</term>, **Coronations** <term>Pattabhisheka</term>, **Weddings** <term>Karagraha</term>, and the **Parting of the Hair** <term>Simanta</term> <note>a pregnancy ritual</note>. By these placements, the fault of the **Detrimental Moon** <term>Ghatachandra</term> is indeed overcome. 

Despite contradictory planetary combinations or the influence of the weekday, and the specific influences arising from each zodiac sign, these faults are discarded in the regions of **Vanga** <note>Ancient Bengal</note> and other specific lands. In the remaining regions, they remain auspicious. 

A marriage remains auspicious even if the couple's signs are in a **Six-Eight relationship** <term>Shadashtaka</term> <note>a traditionally incompatible 6/8 house relationship between the bride's and groom's signs</note> if the following pairings occur: **Leo** <term>Simha</term> with **Pisces** <term>Meena</term>, **Taurus** <term>Vrisha</term> with **Sagittarius** <term>Dhanvi</term> or **Cancer** <term>Kulira</term>, **Aquarius** <term>Kumbha</term> with **Virgo** <term>Kanyaka</term>, and **Scorpio** <term>Kari</term> or **Gemini** <term>Yama</term> with **Libra** <term>Tauli</term>. In these specific combinations, the marriage <note>original: *panigrahah*</note> is favorable, provided the three previously mentioned qualities <term>gunas</term> are found to be compatible. However, a match sharing the **First Single Pulse** <term>Adya-ekanadi</term> <note>the most severe of the pulse/Nadi incompatibilities</note> causes separation.

<summary>This page outlines specific planetary placements (Sun, Mars, Saturn, and Moon) that ensure success in major life rituals and lists regional and zodiacal exceptions that allow a marriage to proceed even during normally forbidden astrological alignments.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for astrological compatibility in marriage, specifically focusing on the Nadi (pulse/channel) compatibility, exceptions for certain relatives, and the dangers of marrying during one's birth month or under one's birth star.</meta>

<page-num>32</page-num>

->**On Compatibility Faults and Exceptions**<-

If both the bride and groom share the **First Pulse** <term>Adya-nadi</term>, it brings about intense suffering. If they share the **Final Pulse** <term>Antya-nadi</term>, it results in the intense grief of widowhood. Therefore, these three <note>referring to the three types of Nadi: First, Middle, and Final</note> must be avoided. In the month of **Margashirsha** <note>typically November–December</note> and the three constellations of **Magha**, and especially in the month of **Jyeshtha** <note>typically May–June</note>, one should make every effort to avoid the marriage of the **eldest son or eldest daughter** <note>original: *jyeshtha-putra-duhitroktam*; a traditional prohibition against "Triple Jyeshtha"—the month, the star, and the child all being Jyeshtha</note>.

If a couple shares the same **Lunar Mansion** <term>Nakshatra</term> but is born in different **quarters** <term>Pada</term>, it is considered auspicious. However, if they are born in the very same quarter of that star, it results in death; such is the teaching of the Sage **Garga**. Where the **Zodiac Sign** <term>Rashi</term> is the same, even if the **Pulse** <term>Nadi</term> is also the same, there is no "Pulse-fault" <term>Nadi-dosha</term> in that case; such a marriage is remembered as one that bestows good fortune.

In the marriage of a **maternal uncle’s daughter** <note>original: *mātulasya sutodvāhe*; referring to cross-cousin marriage traditions</note>, one who is learned in the ancient lore <term>Purana-jna</term> need not consider the Pulse, nor the Temperament <term>Gana</term>, nor the Sign, nor the Sexual Compatibility <term>Yoni</term>. In that specific case, one should consider nothing else except for **Planetary Friendship** <term>Graha-maitra</term>. This has been stated by the sages **Gabhasti**, **Garga**, **Vasishtha**, and others.

Whatever fruits <note>results</note> were mentioned for the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Vratabandha</term>, even greater significance is placed on them during the birth stars in marriage. If a marriage is performed in one’s **birth month**, or when the Moon is in the **birth sign**, or under the **birth constellation** <term>Janma-rksha</term>, it leads to widowhood and loss. Similarly, a marriage held on one's **birthday** or in one's **birth month** results in poverty and sorrow; of this, there is no doubt.

<summary>This page details specific astrological prohibitions for marriage, focusing on Nadi (pulse) compatibility, exceptions for cousin marriages, and the dangers of marrying during one's birth month or under one's birth star.</summary>

<page-num>32</page-num>

The Sage **Vasistha** <note>one of the legendary seven seers (Saptarishi) to whom many astrological rules are attributed</note> states that the day of birth is like a [difficult period], but for the bride and groom, if **Jupiter** <term>Guru</term> is well-placed, the remaining factors are indeed considered praiseworthy even within the birth month. 

->**On the Bride's Entry into the Husband’s Home**<-

Now, regarding the **Bride's Entry** <term>Vadhū Praveśa</term>: starting from the day of the wedding, the bride's entry should take place on an even-numbered day <note>original: *yugme*; traditional astrology often prefers even days for domestic rituals</note> within the first sixteen days. Beyond that timeframe, or up until the fifth year, there is no further restrictive rule. 

When the astrological alignment falls in the **third, eleventh, or seventh houses** <note>original: *tṛtīya-lābhe samasaptake*; these are considered "Upachaya" or houses of growth and partnership</note>, and for others in the fourth house, the entry results in joy, happiness, wealth, sons, good fortune, and the fruit of a good reputation. 

A **Bride's Entry** performed in the first or third year [after the wedding] is the bestower of auspiciousness. In the fifth year, it is also [acceptable]. However, an entry in the second or fourth years may lead to separation, fear, and sorrow. 

In the event of **national upheaval** <note>original: *deśānāṃ viplave*; referring to war, revolution, or civil unrest</note>, or when traveling to one's own home or town, or when arriving from a great distance, there is no need to observe the prohibitions regarding the **Position of Venus** <term>Shukra-dosha</term> <note>refers to "Venus in the face," an astrological taboo where one should not travel in the direction where Venus is currently visible in the sky</note>. 

Whatever she does first, according to the sequence of hours and days, will lead to either good fortune or death, separation or disease. [If timed correctly], the girl will certainly become beloved to her husband and will always remain devoted to him.

<sig>Manuscript Leaf 1</sig>

Jīraṇo <note>likely a fragment of a larger verse or a specific heading</note>

The wise state that for those arriving at the home with a bride who brings joy and wealth, the entry should be celebrated with the auspicious ritual of the **Bride's Entry** <term>Vadhū Praveśa</term>, which enhances the qualities and longevity of the couple. || 1 ||

---------------------------

For these two, whose bodies are purified by the **seasonal rites** <note>original: *ṛtubhik-kṛti*; referring to the traditional purification associated with natural cycles</note>, they describe the marriage altar. Having ascended [the altar], the bride and groom, with unblinking eyes, should look upon each other's faces. The groom, facing East, and the bride, facing West, should be united by the priest in an **auspicious moment** <term>sulagna</term>. One should not perform the ritual **shaving of the head** <term>Muṇḍana</term> [arbitrarily]. For twins or for two sons, the tonsure should not be performed within the same year. Nor should a marriage be performed [for two siblings] in the same year, though some allow it if **three seasons** <margin>Three seasons</margin> have passed. The marriage of a daughter should likewise be performed according to these rules of connection. If one performs the rituals in this way, it leads to the proper fulfillment of the ancestral offerings. 

If one must perform three tonsures, they should be done after the entry and before the departure. Head-shaving is not performed after the tonsure ritual itself. The rules for the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Vratabandha</term> should be followed as prescribed, including the beginning and the end of the vows. The ritual of **clipping the hair** <term>Keśānta</term> <note>a rite of passage marking the first shaving of a young man's beard, often occurring at puberty</note> is said to be similar to the **tonsure** <term>Caula</term> and should be performed in the sixteenth year. The **completion of student vows** <term>Samāvartana</term> <note>the graduation ritual where a student returns home from their teacher's house</note> is also performed then. Those who know the traditions say that these rites are governed by the solar calendar.

<margin>Astrology <note>original: *Jyo* (abbreviation for *Jyotiṣa*); the science of time-keeping and auspicious moments</note></margin> Therefore, regarding the ritual **tonsure** <term>Muṇḍana</term>, one should arrange these for children born of the same mother in separate years. In the case of three or more siblings, one should never give two daughters in marriage to the same man.

Similarly, observing the **strength of the moon** <note>original: *candrabala*; an astrological requirement where the moon's position must be favorable to the individual's birth sign</note>, a man should approach his wife [for procreation] when she is prepared. This is best done during the night, under the influence of auspicious stars such as **Puṣya**, **Mūla**, **Maghā**, or **Revatī**. One should avoid the fourteenth day of the lunar cycle, the end of the fortnight, the **New Moon** <term>Darśa</term>, and the days dedicated to **ancestral rites** <term>Śrāddha</term>. 

For those desiring a son, the union should occur on even-numbered nights. The wise state that the **hair-parting rite** <term>Sīmantonnayana</term> <note>a ritual performed during pregnancy to ensure the mental health of the mother and child</note> and the **rite for begetting a male child** <term>Puṃsavana</term> should be performed when the Moon is in a masculine-named constellation. These should ideally be conducted on a Sunday, Tuesday, or Thursday.

These rites are most effective when malefic planets are situated in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses of the birth chart, and when **Jupiter** <note>original: *Jīva*</note> is in a **cardinal house** <term>Kendra</term> or a favorable **ninth-division** <term>Navāṃśa</term>. On a desired and auspicious day, one should also perform the **birth ritual** <term>Jātakarma</term> at the time of delivery, followed by the naming ceremony...

Then, [the rituals] should occur on the twelfth day, or under **constellations** <term>Nakṣatras</term> that are categorized as **fixed** <note>original: *dhruva*</note>, **soft** <note>original: *mṛdu*</note>, or **upward-facing** <note>original: *uccaiḥ*</note>. This should be done when **Venus** <note>original: *Śukra*</note> is in a **cardinal house** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart, considered pillars of strength</note>, or on a Sunday, Saturday, or Thursday. One should perform these rites under the influence of the **lord of the birth sign** <note>original: *janmādhipena*</note>, in the birth month, and on the specific day of birth.

However, one should not perform auspicious rites for the **eldest son** <term>Prathama Sūnu</term> during the month of **Jyeṣṭha** <note>the lunar month falling approximately in May–June</note>. Similarly, such ceremonies are restricted during the month of **Pauṣa** <note>approximately December–January</note>. At the stated time, the **preparation for the child** <note>likely referring to the gathering of ritual implements</note> should involve [offerings to] the **Adityas** <note>solar deities</note>, **Mitra**, and the **Vasus**. When **Jupiter** <note>original: *Gurau*</note> is situated in the **ascendant** <term>Lagna</term>, and the **benefic planets** <note>original: *śubha-graha*</note> are in moving signs, and the **malefic planets** <note>original: *krūraiḥ*</note> are in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses, then the **first feeding ceremony** <term>Annaprāśana</term> <note>original: *saprāśanam*; the ritual where a child is first given solid food, typically consecrated rice</note> should be performed for the son on an auspicious day.

<margin>In the **constellation** <note>original: *bhe*</note> of this [ritual], if it is performed when the **Moon** <note>original: *candrā*</note> is in the seventh house or the **Sun** is in a cardinal house, or if the **interlocking of planets** <note>original: *kendru-rati*</note> is unfavorable, the child may become afflicted by **wind-disorders** <term>Vāta-roga</term> <note>ailments associated with the 'Anila' or air element in Ayurvedic medicine</note>, and the father may likewise suffer from illness.</margin>

# Astrology
<page-num>34</page-num>
<header>Astrology</header>

<margin>
Astrology
34
</margin>

When the **Moon** <note>original: *vidhau*</note> is associated with **Mercury** <term>Saumya</term> <note>literally "the son of the Moon," considered a benefic planet</note>, it is said to be enlightened and unbreakable. When the Moon is thus endowed with knowledge and placed in the **cardinal houses** <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, it bestows the seven blessings mentioned previously. When **Jupiter** <note>original: *Gurau*</note> is well-placed, it grants a **long life** <note>original: *cirāyur*</note>; when **Venus** <note>original: *Bhṛguje*</note> is well-placed, it grants **fame** <note>original: *yaśo*</note>.

The **Moon** <note>original: *Śaśāṅkaḥ*</note>, when situated in the **eighth house** <note>original: *yavalyeṣṭanda-sthitau*</note>, acts as a **bringer of death** <term>Mṛtyukara</term> <note>this refers to 'Candra-Aṣṭama,' a transit often avoided in elective astrology for beginning new ventures</note>, even if the person possesses stable brilliance and kin. 

The following rituals: **Tonsure** <term>Cūḍākarma</term> <note>the first ceremonial haircut</note>, the **Royal Consecration** <term>Nṛpa-Abhiṣeka</term>, entering a new home, the **installation of a deity** <note>original: *devasthāpana*</note>, and the **Sacred Thread ceremony** <term>Mauñji-bandhana</term> <note>the initiation into Vedic study</note>—these works should be performed during the **Northern course of the sun** <term>Uttarāyaṇa</term>.

However, these rites are forbidden when **Jupiter** <note>original: *Devajyā*</note> or **Venus** <note>original: *Uśanas/Kujito*</note> are **combust** <term>Astam</term> <note>when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen, losing its ritual power</note> or are situated in the house of an enemy. One should wait until they have **risen** <note>become visible again</note>. 

A planet is considered a **child** <term>Bāla</term> <note>too young/weak to give results</note> for a period: **Venus** <note>original: *Śukro*</note> is a child for ten days after rising, and **aged** <term>Vṛddha</term> for five days before setting. Similarly, **Jupiter** <note>original: *Jīvo*</note> is considered "aged" or a "child" for half a month <note>one *Pakṣa* or 15 days</note>. Others say the state of "infancy" and "old age" for these two planets lasts ten days, while some authorities suggest seven nights. When these planets have emerged from the rays of the **Sun** <note>original: *Arkān*</note> and are no longer weak, only then should the rituals proceed.

<summary>This page details the astrological requirements for major life-cycle rituals like the Sacred Thread and Tonsure, emphasizing that Jupiter and Venus must be visible and "mature" (not Bāla or Vṛddha) for the ceremonies to be auspicious.</summary>
<keywords>Cūḍākarma, Mauñji-bandhana, combustion, Jupiter, Venus, lunar houses, electional astrology</keywords>

# Astrology
<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>Auspicious Timings for Rituals</header>

The ritual of the **first haircut** <term>Chudakarman</term> <note>literally "the rite of the tonsure," usually performed in the first or third year of a child's life</note>, as well as the cutting of the beard and nails, should be performed when **Jupiter** <note>original: *ijya*</note> is in a favorable lunar mansion. These actions are forbidden on the **"empty" lunar days** <term>Rikta</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered unlucky for new beginnings</note>, during the month of **Kartika** <note>October–November</note>, and at the transition of day and night.

For the **rite of quickening a male child** <term>Pumsavana</term> <note>a ritual performed in the early stages of pregnancy</note>, the following lunar mansions are beneficial: **Ashadha**, the two **Bhadrapadas**, **Mula**, **Hasta**, **Shravana**, **Ashvini**, and **Pushya**. The preferred days for this rite are **Tuesday** <note>the day of Mars</note>, **Sunday** <note>the day of the Sun</note>, and **Thursday** <note>the day of Jupiter</note>.

The **ritual of parting the hair** <term>Simantonayana</term> <note>a pregnancy rite performed for the mental well-being of the mother and the unborn child</note> should also be conducted on the days of the **Sun**, **Jupiter**, or **Mars**. The auspicious lunar mansions for this ceremony are **Mrigashira**, **Punarvasu**, **Pushya**, **Hasta**, **Mula**, **Shravana** <note>original: *Vaishnave*</note>, the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Rohini**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitre*</note>, **Chitra**, and **Ashvini**.

The **naming ceremony** <term>Namadheya</term> brings good fortune when performed under the stars of **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, **Svati**, **Revati**, **Mrigashira** <note>original: *Shashi-daivate*</note>, **Shravana**, and **Hasta**.

For the **graduation ceremony** <term>Samavartana</term> <note>the ritual marking the end of Vedic studies and the student's return home</note>, one should choose a time when the Moon is perfectly clear and pure. This should be performed on a **Sunday** <note>original: *Aditya-divase*</note> under the stars of **Jupiter**, the **Sun**, **Mercury**, **Revati**, **Anuradha**, the **Uttaras**, or **Rohini**. However, one must strictly avoid the first, eighth, and ninth lunar days <note>original: *navashtami pratipada*</note>, and the ritual should not take place when the zodiac signs of **Aries**, **Scorpio**, or **Leo** <note>original: *Mesham cha Kita-Hari*</note> are rising.

<summary>This page outlines the specific astrological windows—including lunar mansions (Nakshatras), weekdays, and lunar days (Tithis)—required for childhood and developmental rituals like the first haircut, pregnancy rites, naming, and graduation.</summary>
<keywords>Chudakarman, Pumsavana, Simantonayana, Namadheya, Samavartana, Nakshatras, Tithis, Electional Astrology, Hindu Samskaras</keywords>

# Astrology
<page-num>35</page-num>
<header>Auspicious Timings for Rituals</header>

For the ritual of **marriage to the Sun-plant** <term>Arkavivaha</term> <note>a symbolic marriage to the *Calotropis gigantea* plant, often performed by a man seeking to avert misfortune before a third marriage</note>, it should be performed on an auspicious day during the forenoon when the Moon is in the lunar mansion of **Hasta** and it is a **Saturday** <note>original: *shanaishchara*</note>.

### The Establishment of the Sacred Fire
Regarding the **establishment of the sacred fire** <term>Agnyadhana</term> <note>the ritual where a householder formally sets up the sacrificial fires for daily worship</note>: this should be done when the Moon resides in the mansions of **Uttara** <note>referring to the three "Uttara" mansions: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Vishakha**, **Jyeshtha**, or **Revati** <note>original: *antyadvika*</note>. This rite must not be performed on **"empty" lunar days** <term>Rikta</term>. It is favorable on **Mondays** <note>original: *chandra*</note> and **Sundays** <note>original: *arka*</note>. Other suitable mansions include **Pushya**, **Magha**, **Anuradha**, **Rohini**, **Mula**, and **Svati** <note>original: *vayu*</note>. 

If the fire must be re-established, the same rules apply. Some authorities state that the **establishment of the fire** should be performed at the **time of marriage** <note>original: *darakale*</note>. 

<margin>ka =</margin> It is said that at the time of establishing the fire, the presence of **Saturn** is considered. The mansions of **Krittika**, <margin>bha =</margin> **Mrigashira** <note>original: *saumya*</note>, **Rohini** <note>original: *brahmya*</note>, **Pushya**, **Revati** <note>original: *paushna*</note>, and the **Uttaras** are recommended. Ancient leading priests <note>original: *vipramukhyaih*</note> have declared that establishing the fire during the three **Uttara** mansions is the most excellent path.

->**Salutation to Goddess Saraswati**<- 
<note>The scribe invokes the goddess of wisdom before beginning the section on marriage</note>

### Rituals of Marriage
Now begins the section on **Marriage** <term>Vivaha</term>. Marriage is auspicious when the Moon is in the following lunar mansions: **Revati**, the three **Uttaras**, **Rohini**, **Mrigashira**, **Magha**, **Mula**, **Anuradha**, **Hasta** <note>original: *kara*</note>, and **Svati**. 

The favorable **Ascendants** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ceremony</note> for a wedding are **Gemini** <note>Mithuna</note>, **Libra** <note>Tula</note>, **Virgo** <note>Kanya</note>, and **Leo** <note>Simha</note>. 

The recommended **months** for marriage are:
* **Phalguna** <note>February–March</note>
* **Margashirsha** <note>November–December</note>
* **Magha** <note>January–February</note>
* **Ashadha** <note>June–July; original: *shuchayo*</note>
* **Jyeshtha** <note>May–June</note>
* **Vaishakha** <note>April–May; original: *madhava*</note>

<keywords>Arkavivaha, Agnyadhana, marriage timings, lunar mansions, nakshatras, Saraswati, auspicious months, Hindu astrology</keywords>

### The Rite of the Second Entry
Regarding the **second entry into the husband's house** <term>Dviragamana</term> <note>the ceremonial return of a bride to her husband's home after her first post-wedding visit to her parents</note>: the auspicious **lunar mansions** <term>Nakshatra</term> are **Hasta**, **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, the three **Uttara** mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Mrigashira**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *maitra*</note>, **Ashwini**, and **Rohini**. <unclear>Regarding the ritual for her arrival</unclear>, the mansions of **Mula** and **Dhanishtha** are also suitable. (2)

### Rules for Travel
For the **treasury of travel** <note>rules for embarking on a journey</note>, one should avoid the rainy season. The auspicious **ascendant signs** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> for travel are **Aquarius** <note>original: *kumbha*</note>, **Aries** <note>original: *aja*</note>, **Scorpio** <note>original: *ali*</note>, **Virgo** <note>original: *kanya*</note>, **Gemini** <note>original: *manmatha*</note>, **Pisces** <note>original: *meena*</note>, and **Capricorn** <note>original: *makara*</note> when the Sun is positioned favorably.

### The Rites of Pregnancy
Regarding the **rite for a male child** <term>Pumsavana</term> and the **hair-parting rite** <term>Simantonnayana</term> <note>a ritual performed for the health of the mother and the unborn child, usually involving the husband parting the wife's hair</note>: the best mansions are the triad beginning with **Hasta** <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, as well as **Pushya**, **Shravana** <note>original: *shravana* implied by context</note>, the three **Bhadrapada** mansions, **Ardra**, **Revati**, and **Mrigashira**. These should be performed on a **Thursday** <note>original: *gurau*</note>, **Sunday** <note>original: *dinakara*</note>, or **Tuesday** <note>original: *bhaume*</note>, on a day that is not an "empty" lunar day. The **rite for a male child** is particularly auspicious in the **second or third month** of pregnancy, using a **fixed ascendant** <term>Sthira Lagna</term> in the signs of **Sagittarius**, **Taurus**, **Virgo**, or **Pisces**. The **hair-parting rite** should be performed in the **eighth month** of pregnancy. (3)

### The Naming Ceremony
Regarding the **naming ceremony** <term>Namakarma</term>: it is auspicious when the Moon is in **Pushya**, the three **Uttara** mansions, **Anuradha** <note>original: *maitra*</note>, **Mula**, **Mrigashira**, **Rohini** <note>original: *bha*</note>, **Jyeshtha**, **Dhanishtha**, or **Punarvasu** <note>original: *aditya*</note>. This should be performed during a favorable astrological **alignment** <term>Yoga</term> and on an auspicious **lunar day**. It is traditionally performed on the **eighth or twelfth day** [after birth], but not on the eleventh day...

Now, regarding the **Auspicious Timing for Journeys** <term>Yatra</term>: The constellations <term>Nakshatra</term> of **Hasta**, **Pushya**, **Punarvasu**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *maitra*</note>, the three **Uttara** mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Revati**, **Rohini**, **Mrigashira**, **Ashwini**, and **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *anila*</note> are favorable. For a successful expedition, the **lunar month** <term>masa</term> and **lunar day** <term>tithi</term> should be chosen alongside the **ascendant signs** <term>Lagna</term> of **Leo**, **Virgo**, **Libra**, and **Aquarius** <note>original: *kumbha*</note>.

### 5. On Wearing New Clothes
Regarding the **Wearing of New Garments** <term>Vastra-dharanam</term>: The constellations **Revati**, the five mansions starting with **Hasta** <note>original: *kara-panchaka*; these are Hasta, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Anuradha</note>, and **Pushya** are favorable. For men, **Punarvasu** and **Rohini** are also excellent for wearing new clothes and **gold ornaments**. In these same constellations, the ritual of **Eating the New Harvest** <term>Navanna</term> <note>the ceremonial first consumption of grain from a new harvest</note> is recommended. These timings are considered auspicious for dwelling in a house or traveling to holy places like **Dwarka** or **Jagannath**. This tradition has been followed for three and a half manvantaras <note>an immense period of time in Hindu cosmology; this suggests the ancient authority of these rules</note.

### 6. On the First Feeding of Solid Food
Regarding the **First Feeding of Solid Food** <term>Anna-prashanam</term> <note>the rite of passage where an infant eats solid food for the first time</note>: All constellations are acceptable except **Bharani**, **Krittika**, and **Magha**. One should avoid the days of **Mars** <note>Tuesday</note> and **Saturn** <note>Saturday</note>. The **lunar days** <term>tithi</term> of the 6th, 7th, 12th, and the "empty" days <term>Rikta</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th</note> are generally avoided, though some traditions allow the 7th and 12th. For a child, this ceremony should be performed in the **sixth or eighth month** of life. For female children, however...

### 8. On the First Haircut Ceremony
Now, regarding the **Tonsure Ceremony** <term>Chuda</term> <note>the traditional first shaving of a child's head</note>: The constellations <term>nakshatra</term> of **Revati**, **Ashwini**, **Magha**, the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Punarvasu** <note>original: *aditi*</note>, **Mrigashira**, **Jyeshtha**, and the three **Vishwa** <note>the constellations associated with the Vishwadevas, specifically Uttara Ashadha</note>, **Pushya**, and **Shravana** <note>original: *shravana* is implied by *uttarameravau*</note> are auspicious. 

This should be performed when **Jupiter** or **Venus** is visible, during the **bright fortnight**. One should avoid the signs of **Taurus**, **Virgo** <note>original: *stri*</note>, **Gemini** <note>original: *manmatha*</note>, **Sagittarius** <note>original: *chapa*</note>, **Aquarius**, and **Capricorn**. Furthermore, avoid the "empty" lunar days <term>rikta-tithi</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar cycle</note>, the final day of the month, the sixth day, and major festival days. The timing of the child's birth month should also be considered to ensure the **Tonsure** is auspicious. (8)

### 9. On the Commencement of Education
Now, regarding the **Beginning of Studies** <term>Vidyarambha</term>: The favorable constellations are **Revati**, the group of five starting with **Mrigashira** <note>Mrigashira, Ardra, Punarvasu, Pushya, and Ashlesha</note>, the pair of **Shravana** <note>Shravana and Dhanishtha</note>, the **Purva** mansions, and the three starting with **Hasta** <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, as well as **Mula**. 

The ritual is best performed on **Thursday**, **Friday**, or **Sunday**, when the Sun is in the signs of **Sagittarius** or **Pisces**. One should choose the third or fifth lunar day, while strictly avoiding the "empty" days <term>rikta</term> and the sixth day. When the Sun and Jupiter are well-positioned, the effort toward education will meet with sudden and great success. (9)

### 10. On the Sacred Thread Ceremony
Now, regarding the **Initiation Ceremony** <term>Upanayana</term> <note>the "second birth" ritual where a student receives the sacred thread and begins Vedic study</note>: The favorable constellations are **Purva Ashadha**, the three mansions of **Vishnu** <note>original: *hari-traya*; Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note>, **Ashwini**, the three starting with **Hasta**, **Revati**, **Jyeshtha**, **Pushya**, and **Mrigashira**. 

This ritual should occur during the **Sun's northern course** <term>Uttarayana</term> and during the **bright fortnight**. The signs of **Taurus**, **Pisces**, **Virgo** <note>original: *pramada*</note>, and **Sagittarius** are preferred. The days of **Venus**, the **Sun**, and **Jupiter** are best. The second, fifth, and tenth lunar days are considered the most excellent for this initiation. (10)

For the **Tonsure** <term>Chuda</term> and the **Sacred Thread Ceremony** <term>Vratabandha</term>, the previously mentioned constellations are favorable, but one must avoid Saturdays and Tuesdays. For the **Graduation Ceremony** <term>Samavartana</term> <note>the formal return home after completing Vedic studies</note>, Mars should be avoided. For the **Study of Archery** <term>Dhanurvidya</term> <note>refers to the commencement of training in martial arts and weaponry</note>, the days of Mars and Wednesday, and the constellations of **Uttara Phalguni**, **Uttara Ashadha**, **Uttara Bhadrapada**, and **Krittika** are praised. For legal proceedings or **Divine Ordeals** <term>Divya-vidhi</term> <note>ancient methods of determining truth through sacred oaths or trials</note>, the auspicious constellations are **Jyeshtha**, **Shravana** <note>original: *Hari*</note>, **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, **Hasta**, **Ashwini**, and **Punarvasu**. However, one must avoid Saturdays and the "empty" lunar days <term>Rikta-tithi</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar cycle</note>. (11)

### 12. On Ear-Piercing
**Now, regarding the Ear-Piercing Ceremony** <term>Karnavedha</term>: This ritual is auspicious under the constellations of **Rohini**, **Pushya**, the three **Uttaras**, **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitra*</note>, **Mula**, **Mrigashira**, **Chitra**, **Dhanishtha**, **Shravana**, **Revati**, and **Punarvasu**. 
<margin>Word</margin> 
The ceremony should be performed when the Moon is in the zodiac signs of **Pisces**, **Virgo** <note>original: *Stri*</note>, **Taurus**, **Gemini** <note>original: *Manmatha*</note>, or **Aquarius** <note>original: *Ghata*</note>. It is best performed on even-numbered lunar days, or on Mondays, Wednesdays, Thursdays, or Fridays. One should avoid the months of **Chaitra** <note>approx. March–April</note> and **Pausha** <note>approx. December–January</note>, and avoid the period when the gods are in their "sacred slumber" <term>Vishnu-shayana</term> <note>the four-month period of Chaturmasya when certain ceremonies are traditionally paused</note>. (12)

### 13. On Agricultural Activities
**Now, regarding the Commencement of Ploughing and Sowing** <term>Halapravartana</term>: For the cutting and sowing of seeds, the constellations of **Swati**, **Shravana**, **Pushya**, **Hasta**, **Punarvasu** <note>original: *Aditi*</note>, **Anuradha** <note>original: *Radha*</note>, **Magha**, **Revati**, the three **Uttaras**, and **Rohini** are auspicious. These activities are favorable on the days of **Venus** <note>original: *Kavi*</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: *Vidhu*</note>, and when the Moon is in the signs of **Taurus** <note>original: *Go*</note>, **Virgo**, **Pisces** <note>original: *Jhasha*</note>, and **Gemini**. One must avoid the days of **Mars** <note>original: *Kuja*</note> and the **Sun** <note>original: *Arka*</note>. Furthermore, the 12th lunar day, the "empty" days <term>Rikta</term>, and the transition days <term>Parva</term> <note>the 8th, 14th, and 15th days</note> should be avoided. The 2nd and 10th days are considered beneficial for growth. (13)

Also favorable for commerce are **Revati** <note>original: *Pusha*</note>, **Pushya**, **Swati** <note>original: *Samira*</note>, and **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitra*</note>. Specifically for buying and selling, the constellations of **Anuradha**, **Mula**, **Pushya**, **Dhanishtha** <note>original: *Vasavo*</note>, **Jyeshtha** <note>original: *Shakra*</note>, **Shravana** <note>original: *Hari*</note>, **Revati**, **Swati**, and **Hasta** are considered to grant good results, provided they fall on the days of Mercury, Friday, or Thursday <note>original: *Varkhaspati*</note>. (16)

</margin>

The constellations of **Ashlesha** <note>original: *Sarpa*</note>, **Ardra**, the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, **Krittika**, **Bharani** <note>original: *Yama*</note>, **Magha**, and **Vishakha** should be avoided for travel. (27)

...The constellations of **Ardra** <note>original: *Shankara*</note>, **Punarvasu** <note>original: *Aditi*</note>, **Mrigashirsha** <note>original: *Indu*</note>, **Pushya** <note>original: *Taraga*</note>, the three beginning with **Anuradha** <note>Anuradha, Jyeshtha, and Mula</note>, and **Revati** are favorable. Others are considered mediocre. For departure, one should avoid the **sixth** <term>Shashthi</term> and **twelfth** <term>Dvadashi</term> lunar days, as well as the **"empty" days** <term>Rikta</term> <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight</note> and the **"knot" days** <term>Parva</term> <note>Full and New Moon days</note>. The <unclear>leaf</unclear> and middle portions are auspicious. (17) 

The stars beginning with the **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note> are associated with **Saturn** and the **Moon**. If one sets out on the day of **Mars** <note>original: *Kuja*</note>, one should proceed in order but avoid the East when the Moon is in **Mula**. Avoid the South during **Shravana** and related stars. One should avoid the West when the Moon is in **Pushya**, and avoid the North during **Uttara Phalguni** or **Ashlesha** <note>original: *Sarpa*</note>. One may travel under the constellation of **Hasta** on a **Wednesday** <note>original: *Budha*</note>. (18)

### The Elemental Zones (Mandalas)
Now follow the circles of stars starting with the **Fiery Zone** <term>Agneya Mandala</term>: 
The **Fiery Zone** includes **Purva Phalguni** <note>original: *Bhaga*</note>, **Purva Bhadrapada**, **Bharani**, **Pushya**, **Vishakha**, **Magha**, and **Krittika** <note>original: *Vahni*</note>. These produce mild results during **earthquakes** <term>Avani-kampa</term>, the appearance of **meteors** <term>Graholka</term>, and similar portents. 

The **Airy Zone** <term>Vayavya Mandala</term> includes **Shravana**, the two stars following it <note>Dhanishta and Shatabhisha</note>, **Mrigashirsha** <note>original: *Indu*</note>, **Pushya** <note>original: *Taraga*</note>, **Ashwini**, **Uttara Phalguni**, and **Hasta**. Scholars know that the daily fruit here is extremely slow or weak. (19) 

The **Earthly Zone** <term>Mahendra Mandala</term>...

# Auspicious Times

<margin>
Auspicious
</margin>

When the day is joined with the star **Abhijit** <note>an intercalary star used in specialized calculations</note>, along with **Rohini** <note>original: *Brahma*</note>, **Vishakha** <note>original: *Radha*</note>, **Jyeshtha**, **Ashadha**, or the **Uttaras** <note>the three "Later" stars: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, the results are exceedingly great. One should set out for travel during **Revati** or **Uttara Bhadrapada**. The combination with the month of **Vaishakha** brings the most excellent results for **Architecture** <term>Vastu</term> <note>the traditional Indian system of architecture and design</note>. (20) 

In the section regarding the Elemental Zones <note>Mandalas</note>, during days marked by earthquakes or eclipses, if **Pushya** occurs, one should proceed. The three stars beginning with **Purva Bhadrapada** belong to the **Fire** <note>original: *Agneya*</note> zone here; results are slow or weak in other zones. In the division of the pair of zones, they are destructive. Indeed, the **Water** <note>original: *Varuna*</note> and **Indra** <note>original: *Mahendra*</note> zones sometimes yield no auspicious results for these two. (21)

### The Procedure for House Entry and Architecture

Now, the procedure for <term>House Entry</term> <note>original: *Grihapravesha*</note> and <term>Architecture</term> <note>original: *Vastu*</note>: 
After avoiding the stars **Ashlesha**, **Vishakha**, **Magha**, the three **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, and **Bharani** <note>original: *Yamya*</note>, and leaving aside **Wednesday** <note>original: *Jno* - the day of Mercury</note>, perform the house entry; it is auspicious. 

The signs of **Taurus** <note>original: *Go*</note>, **Leo** <note>original: *Simha*</note>, **Scorpio** <note>original: *Ali*</note>, **Aquarius** <note>original: *Ghata*</note>, and **Gemini** <note>original: *Nri*</note> are favorable. The **bright fortnight** <note>original: *Dhavala Paksha* - the waxing moon</note> is highly recommended. For the **Ascendant** <term>Lagna</term> <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the ritual</note>, the signs of **Sagittarius** <note>original: *Chapa*</note>, **Pisces** <note>original: *Mina*</note>, and **Virgo** <note>original: *Vanita*</note> are best, and it should be done during the day. (22)

# Prohibited Combinations and Planetary Rules

<margin>Astrology 38</margin>
One must strictly avoid the <term>Daily Yogas</term> <note>original: *Nityayogas*; these are 27 mathematical combinations of the Sun and Moon's positions</note> known as **Ganda**, **Vyatipata**, **Vishkambha**, as well as **Shula**, **Vaidhriti**, and **Variyan**. These specific combinations, along with those beginning with **Harsha**, are unsuitable for performing actions related to progeny <note>rituals for children</note> and other daily tasks. One must also avoid the <term>Gandanta</term> <note>the "knot-end" or transition points between certain zodiac signs and lunar mansions, considered spiritually unstable</note>, the period of **Bhadra** <note>an inauspicious astrological division or 'Karana'</note>, and the first **Watch** <note>original: *Prahara*; a period of roughly 3 hours</note> of the day in all auspicious and celebratory works.

(23) On **Sunday** <note>original: *Surya*</note>, the following stars must be abandoned: **Bharani** <note>original: *Antakabha* - the star associated with the God of Death</note>, **Magha**, **Ashlesha** <note>original: *Ahinripa* - the Serpent King</note>, and the three sections of **Vishakha**. 
On **Monday** <note>original: *Chandra*</note>, one should avoid **Pushya**, **Vishakha**, **Jyeshtha** <note>original: *Shakra*</note>, and the two **Ashadhas** <note>Purva Ashadha and Uttara Ashadha</note>. On **Tuesday** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>, avoid **Anuradha** <note>original: *Mitra*</note>, **Vishakha**, and **Shatabhisha**. On **Wednesday** <note>original: *Saumya*</note>, avoid **Ardra** and the three stars beginning with **Dhanishtha**. 
Also avoid **Mula**, **Ashlesha** <note>original: *Ahisha*</note>, **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>, **Anuradha** <note>original: *Maitra*</note>, **Dhanishtha** <note>original: *Vasava*</note>, **Bharani** <note>original: *Yamya*</note>, and **Revati**. (24) On **Thursday** <note>original: *Jiva*</note>, 
avoid **Mula**, **Bharani**, **Svati** <note>original: *Anila*</note>, **Magha**, the three stars beginning with **Rohini** <note>original: *Brahma-trayam*</note>, and **Shatabhisha** <note>original: *Varuna*</note>. On **Friday** <note>original: *Kavi*</note>, 
avoid **Ardra** <note>original: *Shiva*</note>, **Jyeshtha** <note>original: *Indra*</note>, the three **Purvas**, and **Revati**. On **Saturday**, avoid **Chitra**, **Bharani** <note>original: *Yama*</note>, **Magha**, and **Shravana** <note>original: *Hari*</note>.

<margin>Timing:</margin> For the king, the combinations of **Ganda**, **Vyatipata**, **Bharani**, and the three **Maghas**, as well as the pair of **Ashadhas** and **Mula**, are not auspicious for the mind. (25) For the sake of the auspicious rites of children, according to those who know the timing of the scriptures, 
these verses have been compiled and arranged here.

<margin>Astrology</margin> ...and fire, the <unclear>sword</unclear>, and the bowls; the group of twenty-seven <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>original: *nakṣatra*; the 27 lunar mansions or star constellations used in Indian astrology</note>.
<margin>40</margin> In each direction, the numbers eleven, eight, four, and three <note>original: *rudra-aṣṭa-veda-guṇāḥ*; using traditional numerical code-words where Rudra=11, Ashta=8, Veda=4, and Guna=3</note> are added in sequence to the <term>Tithi</term> <note>original: *tithi*; a lunar day</note>. These are divided by the "clothes" <note>original: *vastraiḥ*; likely a code for the number nine in this context</note>, and the remainders determine the sequence of the time-cycles.
The <term>Lagna</term> <note>original: *lagna*; the rising sign or ascendant</note> added to the elapsed <term>Tithi</term> is established. By five, seven, the lunar day, the sun, and the eyes <note>numerical codes for 5, 7, the current Tithi, 12, and 2</note>, added to the <term>Vedas</term> <note>original: *veda*; code for 4</note> in sequence. One should avoid the auspicious ones with the remaining nine-part divisions.
Disease, fire, <unclear>kings</unclear>, and thieves; these results, along with death, constitute the **Panchaka** <note>original: *pañcaka*; literally 'the five-fold,' referring to five specific astrological flaws</note>. One should take the <term>Lagna</term> added to the elapsed <term>Tithi</term> and divide that sum by nine. The results may be disease, <unclear>fire</unclear>, <unclear>lotus-pattern</unclear>, or the king.
<margin>Gha</margin> If the remainder is "form" <note>original: *rūpa*; code for 1</note>, there shall be no death from that <term>Panchaka</term>. This applies to service, the affairs of the kingdom, thieves, the protection of the house, and fire.
At the beginning of an undertaking, one may become sickly if it is done during the day in a <term>Panchaka</term> period. **Thus ends the section on the Panchaka.**
