# Auspicious Timing for Ornaments and Weapons

Ornaments and other finery should be worn under the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>original: *nakṣatra*; lunar mansions</note> of Punarvasu, the three Uttaras <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Pushya, Rohini, Ardra, and <unclear>Ashwini</unclear>; this is also favorable on Tuesday.
Those desiring a long life [should follow these rules]. A woman should not wear gold or similar ornaments [at certain times], though Pushya, Ashwini, and Chitra are generally favorable.
For kings, servants, and leaders, the Nakshatras of Jyeshtha, the Uttaras, Revati, Ashwini, Vishakha, and Anuradha are recommended when associated with the Sun, Jupiter, or Venus. When the Sun is in Aquarius or Scorpio, those seeking success [should observe the Moon].
The lunar day and the day of the week are considered: <term>arrows, swords, spears, and knives</term> <note>original: *śara-khaḍga-kunta-chhurikā*</note> should be taken up when the proper days are joined.

The numerical values are: six, nine, five, four, eight, and seven <note>original: *rasa-nanda-śara-veda-vasu-sapta*; traditional Sanskrit number-symbols where Rasa=6, Nanda=9, Shara=5, Veda=4, Vasu=8, Sapta=7</note>. These are multiplied by ten to find the <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>original: *ghaṭikā*; a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> of the days in their proper order. For Mercury, the Sun, and Mars, the measure is seven parts or nine.
For Saturn, Monday, and Friday, half of that value is proclaimed.
In the eleventh quarter of the Sun, as stated in the **words of the sage Garga** <note>original: *Gargasya vacanaṃ*; Garga is one of the ancient legendary seers of Indian astronomy</note>: "Nine faults arise specifically at night, particularly those associated with the Sun, Saturn, and Mars."

### Astrology

Success in one's objectives is achieved in the West on **Thursday** <note>original: *Gurau*; the day of Jupiter</note>, and success is found in the North on **Friday** <note>original: *Bhṛgau*; the day of Venus</note> and **Saturday** <note>original: *Manda*; the day of Saturn</note>. 
Regarding the <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>lunar mansions</note> of Ashlesha <note>original: *Sarpa*</note>, Vishakha, Ashwini, Chitra, Jyeshtha, and the **fourteenth lunar day**: one should avoid the initial <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>; the remaining time is pleasant for travel.
The astrological conjunctions <note>original: *yoga*</note> of **Vaidhriti** and **Vyatipata**—specifically the <term>Mahapata</term> <note>a highly inauspicious astronomical alignment</note>—must always be avoided. For the **Parigha** yoga, the first half of its duration is always forbidden for all types of work. 
In the yogas of **Vishkambha** and **Vajra**, the first six <term>Nadis</term> <note>synonym for Ghatika</note> are avoided, as are the first six in **Ganda** and **Atiganda**. In **Vyaghata**, nine are avoided, and in the **Mula** Nakshatra, five Nadis are considered censured <note>inauspicious</note>.

### Births and Planetary Placements

If a child is born under [specific] stars, they may be raised by another man <note>original: *par-purukha*; this suggests adoption or the child being born into a different lineage</note>. Whether they reside in the house of a friend or an enemy, there will be [different] outcomes. 

Regarding the placement of the Moon in the houses of the Zodiac: 
In the house of **Aries** <note>original: *Mekha*</note>, in **Taurus** <note>original: *Vrishabha*</note>, or in **Gemini** <note>original: *Mithune*</note>; [likewise] in **Cancer** <note>original: *Karka*</note>. One born in **Leo** <note>original: *Simha*</note> becomes a traveler. One in the form of **Virgo** <note>original: *Kanya*</note> is skilled in the arts <note>original: *kalavidi*</note>. [The text continues through] **Scorpio** <note>original: *Vrishchika*</note>, **Capricorn** <note>original: *Makara*</note>, **Aquarius** <note>original: *Kumbha*</note>, and **Pisces** <note>original: *Meena*</note>. These signs [when poorly placed] bring disease to the body and much anxiety.

The Oceans <note>representing the number 4 or 7</note>, the Rudras <note>representing 11</note>, and the Planets <note>original: *Khechara*; representing 9</note>. The Directions, the <term>Vedas</term> <note>representing 4</note>, Dharma, and Wisdom are thus proclaimed. The Eight Elephants <note>representing 8</note>, the Moon <note>representing 1</note>, the Nandas <note>representing 9</note>, Brahma, and the Sky <note>representing 0</note>—these are the numbers used for calculation. 

In the work of constructing a water-wheel <note>original: *rahat-pat*; a device for irrigation</note>, or in binding and finishing manuscripts, and other such tasks—all of these should be carried out [at the proper time]. One must calculate the direction, the watch of the day <note>original: *prahar*</note>, the constellation, the lunar day <note>original: *tithi*</note>, and the day of the week. After dividing the remaining parts of the inquiry, success <note>original: *siddhi*</note> is attained, provided one avoids the <term>Panchaka</term> <note>a period of five inauspicious constellations: Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati</note>.

# 42

<margin>Astrology - 42</margin>
Six, four, three days, three, seven, and eight; the numeration for counting people and answering <note>original: *sankhyana-jana-prashna*; refers to the method of calculating quantities of people or objects in a query</note> horary questions is thus established.

<margin>Misc.</margin> Join the <term>Prashna Lagna</term> <note>the zodiac sign rising at the moment the question is asked</note> with the *Arudha* <note>the sign indicated by the questioner's position or a specific calculation</note> and double the sum. From this, [subtract] the people and divide the remainder; this reveals the name of the planetary day. Either the lord of the Moon’s sign—

<margin>Bh.</margin> —if associated with benefic planets in the quadrants <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, known as Kendras</note>, then whatever is predicted shall be the truth. Otherwise, the result will be the opposite, a falsehood. The extent of <term>Rahu-vara</term> <note>the "Day of Rahu" or the specific timing influenced by the North Node</note> is thus explained: 

From the ascendant: three, five, four, or six; through these, wealth is attained. The answer to the question is multiplied many times, then mixed and divided by seven. The remainder determines the house of that specific moment. 

Under the influence of the Sun, <term>Adityena-vamih</term> <note>original: *Adityena-va-mihih*; likely meaning fire-like intensity or heat</note> occurs. <term>Soma-siddhi</term> <note>success or achievement related to the Moon</note> is produced. Under <term>Bhauma</term> <note>Mars</note>, there is the risk of death, yet also the pursuit of knowledge and the strength of cattle. Through <term>Guru-Shukra</term> <note>Jupiter and Venus</note>, there is profit, but there is no profit under <term>Shanaishchara</term> <note>Saturn</note>. The timing for the Sun is one month, and for the Moon, it is twenty days.

# 43

Under **Mars** <note>original: *Bhauma*</note>, know the period to be three months; under **Mercury** <note>original: *Budha*</note>, it is seven days; under **Jupiter** <note>original: *Jiva*</note>, it is ten and nine days <note>19 days</note>. Under **Venus** <note>original: *Bhrigu*</note>, the period is twenty days, and under **Saturn** <note>original: *Shani*</note>, the result occurs within a year. This is the <term>Prashnatattva</term> <note>literally "The Essence of Inquiry"; a system of horary astrology where the timing and nature of an answer are derived from the moment the question is asked</note>, including the significance of letters and body parts as established by the Great Sages. 

Add the sum of the numerical values and always divide the remainder by eight. If the result falls in an **even sign**, it is a **girl** <note>original: *Kanya*</note>; if in an **odd sign**, it is a **boy** <note>original: *Kumara*</note>. In that <term>Prashna Lagna</term> <note>the ascendant or rising sign at the moment of the query</note>, the **Sun** and **Jupiter**—

<margin>
Nine
Five
</margin>

—along with **Mars** in the third or seventh house, are used to determine the conception of a son. Others say these planets indicate the wealth of the spouse. Double the result of the remaining signs. 

<note>The following section describes the mathematical method for determining the direction of a lost object or a person's travels</note> 

Calculations involving the directions of West and North; if the remainder is seven, it is considered "fixed." Thus, in the *Prashnatattva*, the location may be **East** or **West**, near or far, **South** or **North**. It may be in the **Southwest** <note>original: *Nairitya*</note>, or currently on the road, or it may no longer exist in the fire-pit <note>original: *agnivapake*; likely referring to something consumed or destroyed</note>. When the calculation is divided, the birth is determined from the **Sun** and the remainder of the <term>Navamsha</term> <note>the "ninth-part" harmonic chart used for fine-tuning astrological predictions</note>.

Know that a remainder of **one** indicates fire or heat; a remainder of **five** signifies loss or decay <note>original: *kshaya*</note>; and **three** is considered equal or neutral. If the remainder is **zero** <note>original: *shunye*</note>, the result is void or nothingness.

When the calculation falls in the **fifth**, **ninth**, or **second** positions relative to the village, or in the **tenth** and **eleventh**, it is a giver of happiness and successful results. In the **fourth** house, one should predict a state of staying or stability in the village. The **seventh** house indicates the destruction of one's own objectives; here, the person experiences failure or wandering at every step. 

If the village is situated in the **twelfth** sign <note>original: *dvadasho*; often associated with loss in astrology</note>, wealth that was earned in another place will be destroyed in that location.

According to the **Vayu Purana** <note>one of the eighteen major Hindu Puranas, containing lore on the cosmos</note>: "O best of the twice-born <note>original: *dvija-sattamah*; a term of respect for those of the priestly or scholarly class</note>, there are only three positions for all the planets. I shall designate them here according to the truth: the **Jaragava** is the middle position, **Airavata** is the northern, and **Vaishvanara** is the southern."

Each of these three paths—the middle, northern, and southern—is further divided into three "lanes" <note>original: *vithi*; celestial belts used to track the movement of planets and the Sun</note>. These nine lanes are formed by groups of three **Nakshatras** <note>lunar mansions</note> each, starting with **Ashvini**.

1. **Nagavithi** <note>The lane of the serpent</note>

3. **Airavati** <note>The lane of the celestial elephant</note>

The three lanes of the **Equatorial** <note>original: *vaishuvata*</note> or **Middle Path** are:
1. **Arshabhi** <note>The lane of the bull</note>
2. **Govithi** <note>The lane of the cow</note>
3. **Jaragavi** <note>The lane of the old bull</note>

The three lanes of the **Southern Path** are:
1. **Ajavithi** <note>The lane of the goat</note>
2. **Mrigavithi** <note>The lane of the deer</note>
3. **Vaishvanari** <note>The lane of the sacred fire</note>

The three mansions of **Ashvini**, **Bharani**, and **Krittika** <note>original: *A. Bha. Kri. 3*</note> constitute the **Nagavithi**. **Rohini**, **Mriga**[shirsha]...

This being established, during the **Northern Passage** <note>original: *Uttarayana*; the summer solstice period</note>, the Sun is pulled by the "wind-fetters" <note>original: *vayu-pasha*; cosmic currents imagined as cords</note> tied to the tip of the **Pole Star** <note>original: *Dhruva*</note>. Because of this ascent and its entry into the northern circle, the days increase in length and the nights decrease. 

During the **Southern Passage** <note>original: *Dakshinayana*; the winter solstice period</note>, due to the redirection of these fetters and the Sun's descent, it enters the outer circle; thus, the lengths of day and night are reversed <note>nights lengthen and days shorten</note>. 

At the **Equinox** <note>original: *Vaishuvata*</note>, because the tension in the fetters is equalized, the Sun enters the middle circle at a balanced position. Because the motion is equalized, there is equality between day and night.

For those located to the south of **Mount Meru** <note>the center of the cosmos</note>, starting from the left, from the direction of **Indra** <note>the East</note>, and proceeding toward the Pole Star...

## Astronomy

<margin>Astronomy 45</margin>
For these [celestial lanes], starting from the southern direction, the sequence begins with the East and so forth. For those located in the North, the sequence begins with the West <note>original: *Varuni*; another name for the West, associated with the god Varuna</note> and proceeds to the East. For those in the East, the sequence begins with the North <note>original: *Saumya*; associated with the god Soma</note> and proceeds to the East.

The Sun <note>original: *Ravi*</note>, appearing in a diminutive form, is carried by the seven Vedic meters <note>original: *Chandas*; these are the rhythmic scales of Sanskrit poetry, such as Gayatri, which are personified as the horses of the Sun's chariot</note> in the form of seven horses. He is firmly established upon a wheel that is bound and fixed [to the celestial axis]. The distance from the Earth to the Sun is one hundred thousand <term>Yojanas</term> <note>an ancient Indian unit of distance, traditionally estimated between 8 and 9 miles</note> (100,000).

Above that, the Moon <note>original: *Chandra*</note> is at a distance of one hundred thousand (100,000).
Beyond that are the Constellations <note>original: *Nakshatras*</note> at three hundred thousand (300,000).
Beyond them, Venus <note>original: *Shukra*</note> is at a distance of two hundred thousand (200,000).

Above that, Jupiter <note>original: *Vrihaspati*</note> is at one hundred thousand (100,000).

Above Saturn, the **Seven Sages** <note>original: *Rishis*; the stars of the Big Dipper or Ursa Major</note> are at eleven hundred thousand (1,100,000) <term>Yojanas</term>.
Above them, the **Pole Star** <note>original: *Dhruva*</note> is at thirteen hundred thousand (1,300,000) <term>Yojanas</term>.

- To the Sun: 1 [Lakh]

# 45
## Astronomy and Cosmology

Above [Mars], Saturn <note>original: *Shani*</note> is at a distance of [two hundred thousand] (200,000). Above that, the Seven Sages <note>original: *Rishis*; the stars of the Big Dipper or Ursa Major</note> are at [one hundred thousand] (100,000). Above them, the Pole Star <note>original: *Dhruva*</note> is at [one hundred thousand] (100,000).

The extent of the inhabited regions above the Earth is said to be ninety thousand [Yojanas] (90,000). The <term>Siddhas</term> <note>perfected masters</note> and <term>Vidyadharas</term> <note>semi-divine "wisdom-holders" or celestial musicians</note> dwell at eighty thousand (80,000). Above the Earth, as far as the "swans" <note>original: *Hamsas*; often used metaphorically for high-flying birds or pure souls</note> fly, for a distance of one hundred [Yojanas] (100) is the abode of spirits. Up to that point are the clouds. Above that is the atmosphere <note>original: *Antariksha*</note>, which is the dwelling place of the <term>Pisachas</term> <note>flesh-eating spirits</note>, <term>Pretas</term> <note>hungry ghosts</note>, and the various hosts of <term>Bhutas</term> <note>ghosts or elemental spirits</note>.

Below the Earth, at a distance of ten thousand <term>Yojanas</term> <note>original: *Ayuta*; approximately 10,000 leagues</note>, lies the realm of <margin>Below =</margin> **Atala** <note>the first level of the underworld</note>. Ten thousand Yojanas below Atala is **Vitala**. Ten thousand below Vitala is **Sutala**. Ten thousand below Sutala is **Mahatala**. Ten thousand below Mahatala is **Rasatala**. Ten thousand below Rasatala is **Talatala**.

Below Talatala lies **Patala** <note>the lowest of the seven underworlds</note> at ten thousand Yojanas. Below Patala, <margin>Ananta</margin> at a distance of thirty thousand Yojanas (30,000), resides **Ananta** <note>also known as Shesha, the infinite cosmic serpent who supports the Earth on his hoods</note>.

Now, the measurement of a **Day of Brahma** <note>a *Kalpa*, representing a vast cycle of cosmic time</note>, which is the source of all knowledge, is described. It is said: 

->One thousand cycles of the Four Ages <note>original: *Chaturyuga*; a cycle of four world ages: Satya, Treta, Dvapara, and Kali</note> is called a single Day of Brahma.<-

This is the treasury of all safety and stability.

<sig>Astronomy</sig>

<margin>Astronomy 46</margin> The **Great Cycle** <note>original: *Chakoti*; here referring to the total number of years in a Kalpa, 432,000,000</note> is 432,000,000 years. Here is its consideration: first, the duration of the **Golden Age** <note>original: *Krita Yuga*</note> is 1,728,000 years; the duration of the **Silver Age** <note>original: *Treta Yuga*</note> is 1,296,000 years; the duration of the **Bronze Age** <note>original: *Dvapara Yuga*</note> is 864,000 years; and the duration of the **Iron Age** <note>original: *Kali Yuga*</note> is 432,000 years.

When these four are added together, they become one **Great Age** <note>original: *Mahayuga*</note>. After seventy-one (71) such Great Ages, one <term>Manu</term> <note>a cosmic progenitor who presides over a specific period of time</note> is born. In this way, during a single day of the creator god **Brahma**, fourteen <term>Manus</term> pass away. His night is of the same duration. 

Thirty such days and nights make one month. Twelve such months make one year. In this manner, [Brahma's] life spans one hundred years. 

In this current Iron Age <note>original: *Kali Yuga*</note>, there are six kings who establish their own eras: <term>Yudhisthira</term> <note>the eldest Pandava brother from the Mahabharata epic</note>, <term>Vikrama</term> <note>King Vikramaditya, whose era began in 57 BCE</note>, and <term>Shalivahana</term> <note>the king whose era began in 78 CE</note>. After them comes King <term>Vijayabhinandana</term>, then the King <term>Nagarjuna</term>, and finally <term>Kalki</term> <note>the prophesied final incarnation of Vishnu who ends the Kali Yuga</note>. These six are the kings who define the chronological eras within the Iron Age.

Regarding the characteristics of the **House-Cycle** <note>original: *Griha Chakra*; the astrological houses or the zodiacal wheel</note>: having divided the house into nine parts, five parts are shown... or [the consideration of] planetary friendship... <unclear>tivi</unclear>

### On the Slope of the Land

A slope toward the **East** increases prosperity for men. 
Likewise, a slope toward the **North** bestows wealth. 
A flow toward the **West** causes the depletion of riches. 
A flow toward the **South** <note>literally "the path of livelihood"</note> results in death. 

### Planetary Conjunctions

When **Mercury** and **Venus** <note>original: *Budha* and *Shakra* (here referring to Shukra)</note> are positioned near one another, they can cause the entire earth to become a single ocean <note>a metaphor for heavy flooding or a deluge</note>. If they are not positioned in the middle of the solar path, they may even dry up the oceans.

### Results of the Solar Transit

Now follow the results of the **Solar Transit** <note>original: *Sankranti*; the moment the sun enters a new zodiac sign</note> according to its "vehicle" or mount:
The **Lion**, the **Tiger**, the **Boar**, the **Donkey**, and the **Elephant**. Also, the **Buffalo**, the **Female Dog**, the **Ass**, the **Bull**, and the **Dog**. The life of the Boar... <note>The text here lists the various animal "mounts" that the personified Transit arrives upon, which traditional astrologers use to predict whether the coming month will be violent, prosperous, or lean.</note>

# Bava, Balava, and Kaulava; then know those named Taitila, Gara, and Vanija
## Thus the Noble Ones speak of the seven divisions of time <note>original: *karana*; in Indian astrology, a *Karana* is half of a lunar day (*tithi*). There are 11 Karanas in total, but these 7 are the "movable" ones that rotate through the lunar month.</note>

<margin>Astronomy</margin>
One should understand the attributes of the divisions beginning with **Bava**: a shield, a bow, a balance <note>original: *tula*; scales used for weighing</note>, and a staff. Within the sequence of **Bava** and the other divisions, the following are also assigned: the *bhrushundi* <note>a spiked mace or ancient projectile weapon</note>, the *bhindi* <note>a sling or short javelin</note>, the fire-staff, the heavy club, the *tomara* <note>an iron crowbar or javelin</note>, the spear, the noose, and the elephant goad. These are the specialized tools used here to inspire awe. 

Regarding their vestments, the colors white, red, variegated, yellow, and blue are worn. The specific materials used for clothing—blankets, skins, patched garments, tiger-skins, and bark-cloth—are to be held by the personifications of **Bava** and the subsequent divisions in their proper order. 

As for their food offerings: rice pudding, fermented mash, honeyed milk, fresh milk, curds, spiced rice, jaggery, clear ghee, and sugar are designated for **Bava** and the others. 

The precious materials also follow a specific sequence: ruby, pearl, silver, diamond, hessonite, and coral. Large neck-ornaments, gold, crystal, and cat’s eye gems likewise appear in this order. 

For anointing, one should use musk, sandalwood, saffron, *gorochana* <note>a bright yellow pigment found in the gallbladders of cattle, considered highly auspicious</note>, lac, *vacha* <note>sweet flag</note>, turmeric, "lion’s blood" <note>original: *simha-raktam*; likely a reference to a red resin like Dragon's Blood or a specific medicinal plant rather than literal blood</note>, and agarwood. In the rites of the **Bava** group, sandalwood paste should be applied, along with flowers such as *Punnaga*, jasmine, *Vakula*, and garlands of *Malika*.

<summary>This page catalogs the iconographic and ritual associations—including weapons, clothing, foods, and gems—for the seven movable Karanas (astrological half-days). It serves as a reference for priests to correctly equip and make offerings to these personified time divisions.</summary>
<keywords>Karanas, Bava, Balava, astrological symbols, ritual offerings, Vedic gems, Indian aromatics, Jyotisha</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical and ritualistic analysis of the Karanas (astrological half-days) begun on the previous page. While the preceding text focused on the iconography (weapons and clothes) of the first few Karanas, this section expands on the offerings of flowers, the specific influences of the later "fixed" Karanas, and the ominous predictions related to solar and lunar transitions (Sankranti) and eclipses.</meta>

<margin>Astronomy</margin>

# Offerings and the Fixed Divisions
## On the floral heaps and the results of the Transitions

<gloss>Chai <note>likely an abbreviation for the month of Chaitra</note></gloss> They bear heaps of flowers: the **Japa** <note>original: *japā*; the red Hibiscus</note>, the **Blue Water-lily**, the **Madhavi** creeper, along with **Champaka**, **Patala**, **Kunda**, and **Ketaki**. These are associated with the divisions beginning with **Bava** and the mountains. 

The seven <note>referring to the seven moving Karanas</note> including **Taitila** perform their essence, while the divisions of **Kaulava**, **Shakuni**, and **Kimstughna** <note>these are the 'fixed' Karanas that occur only at the end of the lunar month</note> also exert their influence. 

And <gloss>Dhau <note>likely a scribal abbreviation for a specific calculation or planet</note></gloss> the solar effects produced in **Gara**, **Vanija**, and **Visti** <note>original: *viṣṭi*; also known as Bhadra, this is a division usually avoided for auspicious works</note> are to be noted. In the division of **Balava**, the established rules are written for the sake of kings at the time of their departures. 

### The Results of the Transition
Thus ends the **Results of the Transition** <note>original: *saṅkrāntiphala*; the predictions based on the Sun's entry into a new zodiac sign</note>. 

Regarding eclipses: if a planetary eclipse occurs during the rising or setting of the sun, it signifies the destruction of no small amount of grain. A total eclipse <note>original: *sarvagrasta*</note> occurring in the sky brings the danger of fire. If a lunar eclipse occurs on the fourteenth lunar day, it is particularly significant. 

Without the second part of the **Shakuni** division, between the two halves of the New Moon day... regarding **Go**, **Kimstughna**, and the first day of the month of **Magha**.

<column-break/>

**Written by:** Sadhu Ram <note>The scribe's name</note>
**Location:** The Temple of the Illustrious Lord **Raghunath**, Jammu.
**Date:** Year 1886 of the Vikram Era <note>approx. 1829 CE</note>, during the month of **Ashadha**, on the 28th day of the dark fortnight. 

May there be auspiciousness!

<note>The inscription "E-40" is written in black ink directly on the red fabric cover. The handwritten date "Samvat 1960" corresponds to approximately 1903 CE. The "Extent" field "48 leaves" identifies the manuscript's length. The title refers to a work on the science of astrology/astronomy.</note>

<note>Terminology explanation:
- **Astrology/Jyotisha**: In the Indian context, this is a "Vedanga" or limb of the Vedas, used for calculating time and auspicious moments.
- **Wish-Fulfilling Tree (Kalpataru)**: A common metaphor in Sanskrit titles for a comprehensive reference work.
- **Leaves (Patra)**: Traditionally made of birch bark or paper, these are the individual pages of the unbound manuscript.
- **Samvat**: Short for Vikrama Samvat, the historical Hindu calendar era.
- **Shri Raghunatha Temple**: Located in Jammu, this temple houses a famous collection of Sanskrit manuscripts established by the Dogra rulers.
</note>
